The x64 scanner derives an ntoskrnl export scan range, skips HAL7600's replacement body by finding the preserved `66 90` boundary, and otherwise parses the ordinary x64 Zw redirector sequence. |
| Logic Risk | The old `$Workaround$` label hid the distinction between a scanner exception and a patching strategy. Treating the replacement stub as a legal redirector returns the wrong address. Treating the branch as permission to patch kernel code conflicts with Microsoft guidance that read-only kernel code writes can bugcheck and with the instruction-cache coherency requirement for executable-code mutation. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-097-zw-redirector-hal7600-skip-contract.md` records Microsoft Nt/Zw native-service, Intel instruction-reference, read-only kernel memory, and `FlushInstructionCache` references. `docs/plan/srev-097-zw-redirector-hal7600-skip-contract.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `ZW_REDIRECTOR_HAL7600_SKIP_CONTRACT` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the vague `$Workaround$ - 3rd party fix` labels in `hook_32.c` and `hook_64.c` were replaced with explicit HAL7600 byte-pattern contracts and a statement that the branch is a scanner skip, not a legal Zw redirector shape and not code-patching permission. No runtime behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-097.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, local `Hook_GetService` topology, exact x86/x64 HAL7600 byte-pattern predicates, original-boundary search, stale `$Workaround$` removal, ordinary Zw redirector parsing preservation, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-097.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows x86/x64 matrix with clean ntoskrnl Zw stubs, HAL7600-style modified `ZwLockProductActivationKeys` stubs, Driver Verifier import redirection, and service lookup for both Nt and Zw outputs. |

### SREV-098: IE Embedding CLSID Registry Policy

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | source-level classified after official registry-filter callback, REG_NOTIFY_CLASS, COM CLSID registration, LocalServer32/InprocServer32, and IWebBrowser2 shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/key_flt.c` registers a Vista+ registry callback through `CmRegisterCallbackEx`, handles only `RegNtPreCreateKeyEx` and `RegNtPreOpenKeyEx` for ordinary create/open routing, then conditionally denies opens/creates whose `CompleteName` contains `CLSID\\{0002df01-0000-0000-c000-000000000046}` when `BlockIEEmbedding` is enabled and the caller is a selected host process. Microsoft documents registry callbacks as able to block a pre-notification by returning a non-success `NTSTATUS`. Microsoft documents COM class registration as CLSID registry data used to create class instances, `LocalServer32` as a local-server executable path where COM appends `-Embedding`, and `IWebBrowser2` as implemented by the WebBrowser ActiveX control or an InternetExplorer application instance. Local `comserver9_ie.c` defines this GUID as `CLSID_InternetExplorer`, and `comserver9.c` registers Sandboxie's IE COM server for that CLSID. |
| Data | `BlockIEEmbedding`, `RegNtPreCreateKeyEx`, `RegNtPreOpenKeyEx`, `CompleteName`, InternetExplorer CLSID `{0002DF01-0000-0000-C000-000000000046}`, selected host callers `winword.exe` / `powerpnt.exe` / `excel.exe` / `explorer.exe` / `svchost.exe`, `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED`, and ordinary `Key_MyParseProc_2` sandbox key redirection. |
| Schema | `IE_EMBEDDING_CLSID_REGISTRY_POLICY` says `Key_Callback` handles only create/open pre-notifications for this policy; `BlockIEEmbedding` is a registry lookup denial policy, not a process launch owner; the denied key is the local InternetExplorer CLSID; the policy applies only to selected host callers where `proc` is `NULL`; the selected callers are `winword.exe`, `powerpnt.exe`, `excel.exe`, `explorer.exe`, and `svchost.exe`; the policy returns `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` before ordinary sandbox key redirection; `SearchUnicodeString` is the `CompleteName` matcher and remains null-safe; normal sandboxed registry redirection remains owned by `Key_MyParseProc_2`. |
| Topology | `CmRegisterCallbackEx` installs `Key_Callback`. For create/open pre-notifications, the callback checks the IE CLSID in `CompleteName`, checks `BlockIEEmbedding`, gets the current process name, requires the host-side `!proc` boundary, and returns `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` for selected host callers. If the policy does not deny, host callers pass through and sandboxed callers continue to `Key_MyParseProc_2`. |
| Logic Risk | The old `HACK ALERT` comment made the block look like a generic ForceProcess workaround. The official shape says it is narrower: a registry pre-notification denial that prevents COM from resolving one embedded IE CLSID path. Broadening caller scope or changing COM activation behavior from this source alone would be wrong; that requires a Windows Office/IE runtime matrix. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-098-ie-embedding-clsid-registry-policy.md` records Microsoft registry callback registration, notification handling, `REG_NOTIFY_CLASS`, `REG_CREATE_KEY_INFORMATION_V1`, COM server registration, `LocalServer32`, `InprocServer32`, and `IWebBrowser2` references. `docs/plan/srev-098-ie-embedding-clsid-registry-policy.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `IE_EMBEDDING_CLSID_REGISTRY_POLICY` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the stale `HACK ALERT` wording was replaced with a `BlockIEEmbedding` policy-boundary comment stating that the driver hides the InternetExplorer COM class from selected host callers so COM activation cannot start an embedded IE server path that bypasses Sandboxie's process-launch forcing. No runtime behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-098.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, registry callback registration, create/open pre-notification scope, InternetExplorer CLSID local identity, `BlockIEEmbedding` gate, selected host caller list, `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` before ordinary sandbox redirection, null-safe `SearchUnicodeString`, stale `HACK ALERT` removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-098.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows matrix with Word, PowerPoint, Excel, Explorer, and Svchost host callers; `BlockIEEmbedding` on/off; Office hyperlink open; COM activation trace for CLSID `{0002DF01-0000-0000-C000-000000000046}`; and ForceProcess observation for the resulting normal IE process path. |

### SREV-099: FLT Copied ABI Comment Contract

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | source-level classified after official Filter Manager `FLT_PARAMETERS`, `FltGetFileNameInformation`, `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS`, and preoperation callback buffer-access shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/my_fltkernel.h` is a local copied/subset Filter Manager header kept because the DDK `fltKernel.h` does not compile when `_WIN32_WINNT < NTDDI_VISTA`, which would prevent XP-compatible driver builds. The flagged comments at `FileSystemControl`, `DeviceIoControl`, and `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS` used "broken" wording for ordinary ABI partitioning. Microsoft documents `FLT_PARAMETERS` as the minifilter request-specific parameter union, with method-specific `FileSystemControl` and `DeviceIoControl` arms. Microsoft documents `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS` as a `ULONG` partition for file-name format, query method, and flags. |
| Data | Local copied `my_fltkernel.h`, `FLT_PARAMETERS`, `FileSystemControl.Common/Neither/Buffered/Direct`, `DeviceIoControl.Common/Neither/Buffered/Direct/FastIo`, `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS`, format mask `0x000000ff`, query-method mask `0x0000ff00`, flag mask `0xff000000`, `FltGetFileNameInformation`, `file_flt.c` `Iopb->Parameters`, and `process.c` `FltGetFileNameInformationUnsafe`. |
| Schema | `FLT_COPIED_ABI_COMMENT_CONTRACT` says `my_fltkernel.h` is a local copied `fltKernel.h` subset kept for `_WIN32_WINNT` below Vista / XP driver build compatibility; this SREV does not change the copied FLT ABI layout or numeric constants; `FLT_PARAMETERS` contains method-specific `FileSystemControl` and `DeviceIoControl` union arms; `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS` is a `ULONG` partitioned into name format bits, query method bits, unused bits, and flags; `FLT_FILE_NAME_NORMALIZED` and `FLT_FILE_NAME_QUERY_DEFAULT` remain the local normalized-name query contract; comment wording must not describe official partitioned ABI fields as broken. |
| Topology | WDK Filter Manager ABI defines the legal shape. Sandboxie's local copied header mirrors the subset needed for legacy builds. `file_flt.c` consumes that shape through `CallbackData->Iopb`, `Iopb->Parameters`, and `FltGetFileNameInformation(... FLT_FILE_NAME_NORMALIZED \| FLT_FILE_NAME_QUERY_DEFAULT ...)`; `process.c` also uses `FltGetFileNameInformationUnsafe` with the same normalized/default option pair. |
| Logic Risk | The risk was review drift, not a proved runtime defect. The old wording could make a reviewer treat legal ABI partitioning as broken implementation and then mutate copied WDK structure layout. That would cross the wrong owner boundary. Official API shape says the correct source-level move is only to clarify the comments while preserving ABI layout and numeric constants. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-099-flt-copied-abi-comment-contract.md` records Microsoft `FLT_PARAMETERS`, `FltGetFileNameInformation`, `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS`, and preoperation callback buffer-access references. `docs/plan/srev-099-flt-copied-abi-comment-contract.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `FLT_COPIED_ABI_COMMENT_CONTRACT` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: `broken out into` was replaced with `split into` for FSCTL/IOCTL method-specific union arms, and `broken down into` was replaced with `partitioned into` for `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS` bit sections. No ABI layout, numeric constant, callback registration, or runtime behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-099.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, the copied-header XP compatibility reason, `FLT_PARAMETERS` union arms, `FLT_FILE_NAME_OPTIONS` masks and values, stale `broken out into` / `broken down into` removal, local Filter Manager consumers in `file_flt.c` and `process.c`, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-099.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows/WDK matrix with XP-compatible build settings, Vista+ minifilter load, rename/link callbacks, normalized-name queries, and Driver Verifier observation for copied `FLT_PARAMETERS` layout compatibility. |

### SREV-100: Process Create Caller Snapshot

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official process-create notify, `PS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO`, legacy process notify, and ERESOURCE lock shape; needs Windows delegated-parent process-create runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/process.c` decides whether a new process inherits sandbox state from the creator/caller or the inherited parent. Microsoft documents `PS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO.ParentProcessId` as the inherited parent and says it is not necessarily the process that created the new process; the creator process id is `CreatingThreadId->UniqueProcess`. Before this SREV, the Vista+ notify path passed `PsGetCurrentProcessId()` as `CallerId` even though `CreateInfo->CreatingThreadId.UniqueProcess` was the direct official identity edge. The nearby comment also described the parent lifetime issue as "cause us to crash" instead of naming the actual contract: clone needed sandbox state while `Process_ListLock` still protects the borrowed `PROCESS *`. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/drv/process.c`, `Process_NotifyProcessEx`, `PS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO.ParentProcessId`, `PS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO.CreatingThreadId.UniqueProcess`, legacy `Process_NotifyProcess`, `Process_NotifyProcess_Create`, `CallerId`, `ParentId`, `Process_Find`, `Process_ListLock`, `parent_proc->box`, `Box_Clone`, `Process_Delete`, and `Process_Map`. |
| Schema | `PROCESS_CREATE_CALLER_SNAPSHOT` says `ParentProcessId` is the inherited parent and not necessarily the creator; `CreatingThreadId.UniqueProcess` is the official creator/caller process id for Vista+ create notifications; legacy `PsSetCreateProcessNotifyRoutine` has only `ParentId` and current callback context, so it keeps `PsGetCurrentProcessId` as `CallerId`; `Process_Find(..., &irql)` returns while holding `Process_ListLock` shared; `Process_NotifyProcess_Create` must clone `parent_proc->box` before releasing `Process_ListLock`; `Process_Delete` removes and frees `PROCESS` state only after acquiring `Process_ListLock` exclusive; caller/parent selection must not dereference `parent_proc` after release. |
| Topology | Vista+ create notify now maps `CreateInfo->ParentProcessId` to `ParentId` and `CreateInfo->CreatingThreadId.UniqueProcess` to `CallerId` before entering `Process_NotifyProcess_Create`. The legacy XP notify path keeps `ParentId` plus `PsGetCurrentProcessId()` because that callback shape has no `PS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO`. In the inheritance path, `Process_Find(CallerId or ParentId, &irql)` holds `Process_ListLock` shared, `Box_Clone` copies `parent_proc->box`, the lock is released, and later code uses the cloned `BOX` rather than the borrowed `parent_proc`. `Process_Delete` needs the same lock exclusively before it can remove and free a `PROCESS`. |
| Logic Risk | Parent identity and creator identity are separate data edges. Keeping them distinct matters for AppInfo/RuntimeBroker-style delegated starts, explicit parent-process attributes, and sandbox escape prevention. The local lock discipline already made the borrowed parent pointer safe while cloning; the source comments now state that lifetime contract instead of merely naming a failure outcome. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-100-process-create-caller-snapshot.md` records Microsoft `PsSetCreateProcessNotifyRoutineEx`, `PCREATE_PROCESS_NOTIFY_ROUTINE_EX`, `PS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO`, legacy `PsSetCreateProcessNotifyRoutine`, `ExAcquireResourceExclusiveLite`, and ERESOURCE references. `docs/plan/srev-100-process-create-caller-snapshot.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `PROCESS_CREATE_CALLER_SNAPSHOT` contract. |
| Fix | `Process_NotifyProcessEx` now passes `CreateInfo->CreatingThreadId.UniqueProcess` as `CallerId` to `Process_NotifyProcess_Create`; the debug comment was updated to say `caller`; the misleading static prototype parameter name was changed from `ParentId` to `Process`; the stale "cause us to crash" wording was replaced by a stable sandbox-state snapshot contract. The XP notify path, process-list lock order, and `Box_Clone` timing are unchanged. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-100.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, Vista+ caller identity source, legacy caller identity preservation, `Process_Find` shared-lock return shape, parent box clone before `Process_ListLock` release, `Process_Delete` exclusive removal/free topology, stale crash wording removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-100.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows process-create matrix with ordinary parent create, explicit parent-process attribute create, AppInfo/RuntimeBroker-style delegated create, sandboxed caller with non-sandbox inherited parent, non-sandbox caller with sandbox inherited parent, rapid parent exit during child creation, and Driver Verifier observation for process-list lock balance. |

### SREV-101: Syscall Disabled Skip And Procmon Guard

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | source-level classified after official Nt/Zw native-service, `ZwMapViewOfSection`, section-object, and `NtQuerySystemInformation` shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/syscall.c` enumerates NTDLL `Zw*` exports, derives syscall indexes and kernel addresses, and stores `SYSCALL_ENTRY` records. Two uncovered `$Workaround$` comments were attached to commented-out `YieldExecution` and `MapViewOfSection` skip branches, meaning they were historical notes rather than active skip policy. A third uncovered comment in `Syscall_QuerySystemInfo_SupportProcmonStack` described the local `0xb9` `QuerySystemInformation` guard as a crash outcome. Microsoft documents native `Nt`/`Zw` services as syscall-accessible native services, documents section mapping through `ZwMapViewOfSection`, and warns that `NtQuerySystemInformation` may be altered or unavailable in future Windows versions. |
| Data | `Syscall_Init`, NTDLL `Zw*` export enumeration, `Syscall_GetIndexFromNtdll`, `Syscall_GetKernelAddr`, `SYSCALL_ENTRY`, inactive `YieldExecution` skip comment, inactive `MapViewOfSection` skip comment, `Syscall_Set3("QuerySystemInformation", Syscall_QuerySystemInfo_SupportProcmonStack)`, `user_args[0] == 0xb9`, and Procmon stack support return policy. |
| Schema | `SYSCALL_DISABLED_SKIP_AND_PROCMON_GUARD` says `Syscall_Init` enumerates NTDLL `Zw` exports and builds the Sandboxie syscall table; `YieldExecution` and `MapViewOfSection` compatibility skip branches are intentionally inactive comments; inactive skip branches must not be described as active third-party workaround policy; `MapViewOfSection` hook policy must not be changed from stale Chrome `wow_helper` comments alone; `QuerySystemInformation` is registered through `Syscall_Set3`; `SystemInformationClass 0xb9` is a private runtime guard value and not a public `SYSTEM_INFORMATION_CLASS` contract; this SREV does not change syscall hook registration, skip behavior, or return policy. |
| Topology | `Syscall_Init` loads NTDLL, scans `Zw*` exports, derives syscall metadata, and builds `Syscall_Table`. The inactive `YieldExecution` and `MapViewOfSection` branches remain comments, so valid syscall entries still proceed through ordinary table construction. `QuerySystemInformation` is separately registered with a support predicate that returns `FALSE` for class `0xb9` before Procmon stack support proceeds. |
| Logic Risk | The old `$Workaround$` suffix made inactive comments look like active compatibility policy. Re-enabling either skip branch would be a behavior change requiring Windows runtime proof. For the `0xb9` guard, the official boundary is weaker than the local observation: Microsoft documents `NtQuerySystemInformation` as variable, while the class value remains private/local and must stay runtime-gated. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-101-syscall-disabled-skip-and-procmon-guard.md` records Microsoft Nt/Zw native-service, `ZwMapViewOfSection`, section-object, `NtQuerySystemInformation`, and `ZwQuerySystemInformation` references. `docs/plan/srev-101-syscall-disabled-skip-and-procmon-guard.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `SYSCALL_DISABLED_SKIP_AND_PROCMON_GUARD` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the two disabled skip branches now explicitly say they are historical and intentionally inactive; the `0xb9` Procmon stack guard now says the x86-only exploit can destabilize x64 context rather than presenting a broad crash claim. No syscall hook registration, skip behavior, handler return value, or runtime behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-101.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, NTDLL `Zw*` enumeration topology, inactive YieldExecution and MapViewOfSection branches, `Syscall_Set3("QuerySystemInformation", ...)` wiring, class `0xb9` guard behavior, stale `$Workaround$ and crash wording removal from `syscall.c`, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-101.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows x86/x64 syscall-hook matrix with McAfee ICD-10607, Chrome `wow_helper`, `NtMapViewOfSection`, `NtYieldExecution`, Procmon stack capture, `NtQuerySystemInformation(0xb9)`, HVCI on/off, and Driver Verifier observation for syscall table and hook dispatch stability. |

### SREV-102: Syscall64 Private Table Scanner Boundary

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | source-level classified after official Microsoft KVA Shadow / KB4056892, Intel Rogue Data Cache Load, Windows ARM64 ABI, and ARM64 WDK shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/syscall_64.c` discovers 64-bit service tables by using the exported `KeServiceDescriptorTable` when available, otherwise scanning `KeAddSystemServiceTable` private instruction patterns. The uncovered Meltdown comment sat above a disabled x64 `0x40` / `0xC0` table-spacing check. Microsoft documents KVA Shadow as the Windows mitigation for CVE-2017-5754 / Meltdown, says it changes x64 trap and system-service dispatch internals, and warns drivers must not depend on those internals without updated documentation. The uncovered ARM64 `TODO` sat in the filter-table scanner branch, which currently returns `0` and lets `Syscall_GetServiceTableFilter` fail closed. |
| Data | `Syscall_GetMasterServiceTable`, `KeServiceDescriptorTable`, `KeAddSystemServiceTable`, disabled x64 spacing check, `MasterTable`, `ShadowTable`, `ShadowTable->Addrs`, `Dyndata_Config.ServiceTable_offset`, ARM64 `ADRP` / `ADD` master-table scanner, ARM64 `KeServiceDescriptorTableFilter` lookup branch, and `Syscall_GetServiceTableFilter` fail-closed path. |
| Schema | `SYSCALL64_PRIVATE_TABLE_SCANNER_BOUNDARY` says `Syscall_GetMasterServiceTable` uses exported `KeServiceDescriptorTable` when available before private pattern scanning; the disabled x64 `0x40` / `0xC0` spacing check is a historical private invariant and must not be re-enabled from source comments alone; KVA Shadow / KB4056892 changed x64 kernel-entry and system-service dispatch internals for Meltdown mitigation; Microsoft says those details are subject to change; `Syscall_GetServiceTable` still validates `ShadowTable->Addrs` against `MasterTable->Addrs`; ARM64 filter-table lookup remains fail-closed until a version-gated pattern is proven; this SREV does not change service table offsets, scanner behavior, or dispatch. |
| Topology | x64 path: `MmGetSystemRoutineAddress("KeServiceDescriptorTable")` -> exported table if present -> otherwise scan `KeAddSystemServiceTable` -> derive `MasterTable` -> derive `ShadowTable` by OS-build offsets -> require matching `Addrs`. ARM64 path: master-table lookup scans `KeAddSystemServiceTable` for `ADRP` / `ADD`; filter-table lookup is intentionally fail-closed today and logs `FILTER TABLE` through the caller. |
| Logic Risk | The old `broken` wording invited the wrong repair: re-enable or reshape a private kernel invariant after a mitigation specifically changed kernel-entry internals. The official boundary is weaker: runtime-version-gated scanner evidence is required. The old ARM64 `TODO` similarly invited x64-pattern analogy into ARM64 without Windows ARM64 runtime evidence. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-102-syscall64-private-table-scanner-boundary.md` records Microsoft KVA Shadow, KB4072698 / KB4056892 speculative-execution guidance, Intel Rogue Data Cache Load, Windows ARM64 ABI, and ARM64 WDK references. `docs/plan/srev-102-syscall64-private-table-scanner-boundary.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `SYSCALL64_PRIVATE_TABLE_SCANNER_BOUNDARY` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the disabled spacing check now says KVA Shadow / KB4056892 changed x64 kernel-entry layout enough that the private invariant is not legal; the ARM64 filter-table placeholder now says lookup remains fail-closed until a version-gated `KeAddSystemServiceTable` pattern is proven on real Windows ARM64 builds. No behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-102.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, export-first table lookup, disabled spacing-check shape, post-derivation `ShadowTable->Addrs` validation, ARM64 master-table `ADRP` / `ADD` pattern, ARM64 filter-table fail-closed wording, stale `broken` and `TODO` wording removal from `syscall_64.c`, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-102.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows x64/ARM64 matrix with Windows 7 x64 pre/post KB4056892, Windows 10/11 x64 with KVA Shadow on/off where applicable, Windows ARM64 supported WDK builds, service table discovery, filter table discovery, Driver Verifier, HVCI on/off, and syscall dispatch smoke for ordinary NT and win32k syscall entries. |

### SREV-103: Win32k Current Thread GUI State

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official `IsGUIThread`, thread desktop connection, `PsGetCurrentThread`, and public kernel DDI index shape; needs Windows GUI-thread / direct win32k dispatch runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/syscall_win32.c` directly dispatches win32k syscalls through `Sbie_InvokeSyscall_asm` after temporarily setting the thread token. The uncovered todo proposed calling private kernel conversion routines. Microsoft documents public GUI conversion through `IsGUIThread(TRUE)` for the calling thread, documents desktop connection as assigned to the thread making the connection, and documents `PsGetCurrentThread` as the current executive thread object. The public kernel DDI index does not document `PsConvertToGuiThread` or `KiConvertToGuiThread` as driver-callable DDIs. The old direct-dispatch guard used `PsGetProcessWin32Process(PsGetCurrentProcess())`, which proves process-level Win32 state but not current-thread Win32 state. |
| Data | `Syscall_Api_Invoke32`, win32k syscall indexes with `0x1000`, `Thread_SetThreadToken`, `handler1_func`, `Syscall_Invoke32`, `Sbie_InvokeSyscall_asm`, `PsGetProcessWin32Process(PsGetCurrentProcess())`, `PsGetThreadWin32Thread(PsGetCurrentThread())`, `STATUS_INVALID_ADDRESS`, trap-frame save/restore, and `Thread_ClearThreadToken`. |
| Schema | `WIN32K_CURRENT_THREAD_GUI_STATE` says `IsGUIThread` documents GUI state and optional conversion for the calling thread; Thread Connection to a Desktop assigns a desktop to the thread making the connection; `PsGetCurrentThread` returns the executive thread object for the current thread; `PsConvertToGuiThread` and `KiConvertToGuiThread` are not documented WDK DDIs and must not be called directly from this driver path; `Syscall_Api_Invoke32` direct win32k dispatch must use a current-thread Win32 state guard, not a process-level Win32 state guard; the source change keeps token setting, handler dispatch, direct syscall invocation, trap-frame restoration, and `STATUS_INVALID_ADDRESS` failure behavior intact. |
| Topology | `Syscall_Api_Invoke32` validates the sandboxed caller and win32k syscall index, may set the thread token, probes user arguments, takes `handler1_func` when owned by a handler, and otherwise requires `PsGetThreadWin32Thread(PsGetCurrentThread())` before `Syscall_Invoke32`. Direct dispatch still chooses `ntos_func2` only for configured filter-table use, otherwise `ntos_func`; the failure branch remains `STATUS_INVALID_ADDRESS`; cleanup still clears thread impersonation when needed. |
| Logic Risk | The stale todo named an internal conversion mechanism but not a legal driver boundary. Implementing it literally would add private kernel calls. The current source risk was that the guard checked process Win32 initialization even though the public GUI model and the local todo are both thread-shaped. A process can have Win32 state while a particular thread still lacks Win32 thread state, so direct win32k dispatch should be gated on the current thread. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-103-win32k-current-thread-gui-state.md` records Microsoft `IsGUIThread`, Thread Connection to a Desktop, `PsGetCurrentThread`, and public kernel DDI index references. `docs/plan/srev-103-win32k-current-thread-gui-state.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `WIN32K_CURRENT_THREAD_GUI_STATE` contract. |
| Fix | `Syscall_Api_Invoke32` now requires `PsGetThreadWin32Thread(PsGetCurrentThread())` before direct win32k dispatch. The stale todo and obsolete `MmIsAddressValid` note were replaced with a private-boundary comment. No private GUI-thread conversion call, syscall table discovery change, handler change, or token cleanup change was added. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-103.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, current-thread GUI shape, private-call todo removal, guard replacement, preservation of dispatch/failure topology, supporting local declaration/comment evidence, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-103.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows x64/x86 matrix with sandboxed non-GUI current thread in a Win32-initialized process, sandboxed GUI current thread, `OpenWinClass` / win32k hook paths, `UseWin32kFilterTable` on/off, HVCI on/off, Driver Verifier, and observation that non-GUI current threads return `STATUS_INVALID_ADDRESS` rather than reaching direct win32k dispatch. |

### SREV-104: Token SID Storage Copy Boundary

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official SID, `RtlValidSid`, `RtlLengthSid`, `RtlCopySid`, `SID_AND_ATTRIBUTES`, `TOKEN_USER`, `TOKEN_GROUPS`, `SeFilterToken`, and `SeQueryInformationToken` shape; needs Windows token SID storage runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/token.c` `Token_RestrictHelper1` temporarily rewrites a filtered token's user SID through private `TOKEN.UserAndGroups` offsets before calling `SeFilterToken` again. The uncovered comments described Windows 8.1 shared SID storage and too-small system-process SID storage as workarounds. Microsoft documents `SID` as variable-length and says drivers must use support routines rather than editing SID fields directly; `RtlLengthSid` has undefined output for invalid SIDs and requires `RtlValidSid` first; `RtlCopySid` copies into a caller-allocated destination with an explicit byte length and returns `STATUS_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL` when the destination is too small. |
| Data | `Token_RestrictHelper1`, `TempNewTokenObject`, `Dyndata_Config.UserAndGroups_offset`, `SidAndAttrsInToken->Sid`, `Token_IsSharedSid_W8`, `proc->SandboxieLogonSid`, `AnonymousLogonSid`, `OrigTokenSid`, `RtlValidSid`, `RtlLengthSid`, `RtlCopySid`, `Sbie_SepFilterToken_KernelMode`, `Sbie_SeFilterToken_KernelMode`, and `ObDereferenceObject(TempNewTokenObject)`. |
| Schema | `TOKEN_SID_STORAGE_COPY_BOUNDARY` says SIDs are variable-length and must not be field-mutated directly; both the token SID and `SandboxieLogonSid` must pass `RtlValidSid` before `RtlLengthSid`; inline token SID rewrite uses `RtlCopySid` with the measured destination SID length; `Token_IsSharedSid_W8` is only a private offset-based classifier for whether inline copy is allowed; shared or too-small storage uses pointer substitution and restores the original pointer before the temporary token object is dereferenced; `TOKEN_USER` is not itself a `SeFilterToken` input, while `TOKEN_GROUPS` can carry a user SID as deny-only input. |
| Topology | `Token_RestrictHelper1` creates `TempNewTokenObject` with extra variable storage, finds `SidAndAttrsInToken` through `UserAndGroups_offset`, validates `SidInToken`, defaults `SandboxieLogonSid` to `AnonymousLogonSid` when configured, validates the replacement SID, measures both SID lengths, then either substitutes the SID pointer for shared/short storage or copies with `RtlCopySid(TokenSidLength, SidInToken, proc->SandboxieLogonSid)`. If pointer substitution was used, the original pointer is restored after `Sbie_SeFilterToken_KernelMode` and before `ObDereferenceObject(TempNewTokenObject)`. |
| Logic Risk | The old code only checked `SidInToken[1] >= 1`, then called `RtlLengthSid` on token and replacement SIDs and used raw `memcpy` for inline rewrite. That allowed private-offset drift or malformed replacement SID state to feed undefined SID length into a token object write. The corrected rule is data-shaped: valid SIDs first, measured capacity second, support-routine copy only when inline storage is legal and large enough. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-104-token-sid-storage-copy-boundary.md` records Microsoft `SID`, `RtlValidSid`, `RtlLengthSid`, `RtlCopySid`, `SID_AND_ATTRIBUTES`, `TOKEN_USER`, `TOKEN_GROUPS`, `SeFilterToken`, and `SeQueryInformationToken` references. `docs/plan/srev-104-token-sid-storage-copy-boundary.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `TOKEN_SID_STORAGE_COPY_BOUNDARY` contract. |
| Fix | `Token_RestrictHelper1` now validates `SidInToken` and `proc->SandboxieLogonSid` with `RtlValidSid` before length calculation; stores both measured lengths once; keeps pointer substitution for `Token_IsSharedSid_W8` or insufficient inline destination bytes; and replaces raw inline `memcpy` with `RtlCopySid(TokenSidLength, SidInToken, proc->SandboxieLogonSid)`. The temporary token pointer restore, `SepFilterToken` / `SeFilterToken` call ordering, and restricted-SID cleanup topology are unchanged. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-104.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, SID validation before length calculation, `RtlCopySid` replacing the old inline `memcpy`, pointer substitution remaining only for shared or too-small storage, original pointer restore, stale uncovered workaround wording removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-104.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows token matrix covering Windows 7, Windows 8.1 before/after KB2919355, Windows 10, a configured SbieLogin SID longer than the original token SID, default AnonymousLogon, system-process token application, `Token_IsSharedSid_W8` true/false observations, Driver Verifier, and token object dereference/restore stability. |

### SREV-105: Verify Legacy Certificate Level Compatibility

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | source-level classified after official certificate validity-window and Windows time-value shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/verify.c` parses the local signed-text `Certificate.dat` file and maps `TYPE`, `LEVEL`, `DATE`, `DAYS`, and `OPTIONS` into `Verify_CertInfo`. The uncovered TODO comments suggested removing `LARGE`, `MEDIUM`, and `SMALL` legacy level cases after calendar dates that are now in the past. Microsoft documents certificate validity as a reference-time comparison against `NotBefore` / `NotAfter` style validity boundaries and documents Windows system time as UTC 100-nanosecond intervals. This Sandboxie path is not an X.509 chain parser; its local policy keeps legacy signed level aliases and enforces validity through the common `expiration_date`, `expired`, `outdated`, `grace_period`, and `active` gate. |
| Data | `KphValidateCertificate`, `Certificate.dat`, `cert_date`, `level`, `days`, `Verify_CertInfo`, scheme 1.1 personal / Patreon `LARGE`, `MEDIUM`, `SMALL`, pre-2022 `LARGE` `-2` sentinel, `expiration_date`, `KphGetDateInterval`, `CERT_IS_SUBSCRIPTION`, `BuildDate`, `UtcTime`, `expired`, `outdated`, `grace_period`, `active`, and `STATUS_ACCOUNT_EXPIRED`. |
| Schema | `VERIFY_LEGACY_CERT_LEVEL_COMPATIBILITY` says certificate validity is a time-window decision; Windows system time is represented as UTC 100-nanosecond intervals; `Certificate.dat` is a local signed text contract rather than an X.509 certificate chain; legacy `LARGE`, `MEDIUM`, and `SMALL` levels are parser compatibility aliases for already signed certificate text; `LARGE` assigns an explicit two-year expiration unless it is the pre-2022 semi-perpetual sentinel; `MEDIUM` uses the common default one-year expiration gate; `SMALL` maps to Home subscription before that common gate; subscription certificates are gated by `expired`, non-subscription certificates are gated by `outdated`, and inactive certificates become `STATUS_ACCOUNT_EXPIRED` outside grace period. |
| Topology | `Certificate.dat` signed tags feed `type`, `level`, `cert_date`, `days`, and `options`; scheme 1.1 level aliases map legacy text to local type/level/expiration state; option defaults are derived from level; `expiration_date` is finalized; `CERT_IS_SUBSCRIPTION` selects the active decision edge; the common gate sets `expired`, `outdated`, `grace_period`, `active`, and `STATUS_ACCOUNT_EXPIRED`. |
| Logic Risk | Removing the stale TODO branches would be a compatibility-breaking parser change rather than a validity fix. Old signed certificate text would stop mapping through the same local type/level path before diagnostics and common expiry enforcement. The legal minimal repair is to keep the aliases and replace stale removal-date comments with the compatibility contract. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-105-verify-legacy-cert-level-compatibility.md` records Microsoft `CertVerifyTimeValidity`, `CERT_INFO`, `KeQuerySystemTimePrecise`, `RtlTimeFieldsToTime`, and `RtlTimeToTimeFields` references. `docs/plan/srev-105-verify-legacy-cert-level-compatibility.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `VERIFY_LEGACY_CERT_LEVEL_COMPATIBILITY` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the three stale TODO comments were replaced with legacy compatibility comments that point to the common expiration enforcement below. No certificate parser branch, type/level mapping, option defaulting, expiration calculation, grace-period handling, lock-refresh handling, or return status behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-105.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, legacy level branches, pre-2022 sentinel behavior, explicit `LARGE` two-year interval, `MEDIUM` and `SMALL` default-expiration topology, subscription vs non-subscription gate, stale TODO removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-105.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows build matrix with signed `Certificate.dat` samples for `LARGE`, `MEDIUM`, `SMALL`, pre-2022 `LARGE`, `HUGE`, `STANDARD`, subscription and non-subscription types, expired/non-expired dates, grace-period boundaries, `BuildDate` outdated checks, and lock-refresh enabled and disabled. |

### SREV-106: Low Inject ARM64EC Syscall Entrypoint

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | source-level classified after official Arm64EC ABI, FFS, and Nt/Zw native-service shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/low/inject.c` chooses injection-time pointers for `NtProtectVirtualMemory`, `NtRaiseHardError`, and `NtDeviceIoControlFile` after SbieLow syscall state has been prepared. The uncovered comment said FFS resolution "does not work for syscalls". Microsoft documents Arm64EC interoperability, call checkers, exit thunks, and fast-forward sequences for ordinary hybrid exports; Microsoft also documents native system services as Nt/Zw routines reached by user-mode applications through system calls. Local source shows syscall exports are not ordinary FFS-only exports in this path: `lowlevel_inject.c` captures native Nt* entrypoints and EC syscall wrapper metadata, `init.c` installs `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC` / `EcExitThunkPtr` state, and `inject.c` uses the pre-captured native/EC entrypoints for injection-time calls. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/low/inject.c`, `InitInject`, `INJECT_DATA`, `SBIELOW_DATA`, `data->flags.is_wow64`, `FindDllExport(ntdll_base, ...)`, `Hook_GetFFSTarget`, `NativeNtProtectVirtualMemory`, `NativeNtRaiseHardError`, `NtDeviceIoControlFile`, `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, `EcExitThunkPtr`, `SbieDll_GetEcExitThunkPtr`, `NATIVE_FUNCTION_NAMES`, and `DetourFunc`. |
| Schema | `LOW_INJECT_ARM64EC_SYSCALL_ENTRYPOINT` says ARM64EC allows x64 and Arm64EC code to interoperate in one process; ARM64EC indirect calls use call checkers and exit thunks; fast-forward sequences are small x64 functions that transfer execution to the real Arm64EC function; ordinary ARM64EC exports may be FFS-resolved to native EC targets; native system services use Nt/Zw entrypoints and user-mode applications reach them through system calls; Nt* syscall exports in SbieLow are routed through local syscall-wrapper state rather than ordinary FFS-only export resolution; WOW64 injection resolves 32-bit ntdll Nt* exports from `ntdll_wow64_base`; native and ARM64EC injection use pre-captured native or EC entrypoints from `SBIELOW_DATA`; `NtDeviceIoControlFile` may point to the EC wrapper installed by `PrepSyscalls`. |
| Topology | `lowlevel_inject.c` captures ntdll base, native Nt* entrypoints, and syscall data; ARM64EC appends EC syscall wrapper data and `EcExitThunkPtr`; `init.c` prepares SbieLow syscall wrapper state and may point `data->NtDeviceIoControlFile` at `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`; `inject.c` resolves 32-bit ntdll exports for WOW64 targets but uses `SBIELOW_DATA` native/EC entrypoints for native / ARM64EC targets; `DetourFunc` then restores the hook and loads `SbieDll.dll`. |
| Logic Risk | The old wording made the rule sound like a failed FFS implementation detail. The real boundary is that ordinary hybrid exports and Nt* syscall entrypoints belong to different local owners. Retrying FFS parsing for syscall exports would bypass the SbieLow syscall-wrapper state prepared from driver/runtime data. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-106-low-inject-arm64ec-syscall-entrypoint.md` records Microsoft Arm64EC, Arm64EC ABI, `hybrid_patchable` / FFS, Nt/Zw native-service, and public kernel DDI index references. `docs/plan/srev-106-low-inject-arm64ec-syscall-entrypoint.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `LOW_INJECT_ARM64EC_SYSCALL_ENTRYPOINT` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the stale "does not work for syscalls" wording now states that ordinary ARM64EC exports can be FFS-resolved, while Nt* syscall exports are routed through SbieLow syscall-wrapper state and keep the pre-captured native/EC entrypoints for injection-time calls. No pointer assignment, FFS parsing, syscall wrapper construction, hook target, detour patch size, DLL load, or error-reporting behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-106.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, WOW64 export path, native / ARM64EC pre-captured entrypoint path, local EC syscall-wrapper preparation, FFS helper boundary, stale "does not work" wording removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-106.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows on Arm matrix with ARM64EC target process, x64-emulated target process, WOW64 target process where applicable, `NtProtectVirtualMemory` hook restore, `NtRaiseHardError` injection failure path, `NtDeviceIoControlFile` driver call path, FFS-resolved ordinary exports, SbieLow syscall hook enabled and disabled, and Driver Verifier / loader-lock observation during early process initialization. |

### SREV-107: DriverAssist Host Inject Restart Coalescing

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official SCM enumeration, service open/control/start, and Sleep timing shape; needs Windows host-injected service restart runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/DriverAssist.cpp` receives `SVC_CONFIG_UPDATED`, refreshes ini/syscall state, and then gates host-injected service restart work. The old `JobCounter` comment promised that the first caller waited until the last refresh before starting the job, but later callers incremented and immediately decremented the counter, so this was not a real quiet-window coalescer. The wrapped operation enumerates active Win32 services and may call `ControlService` / `StartServiceW`; Microsoft documents SCM control notifications as serial and says `ControlService` and `StartServiceW` can block for 30 seconds while another service is busy handling a control code. |
| Data | `DriverAssist::MsgWorkerThread`, `SVC_CONFIG_UPDATED`, `SbieIniServer::NotifyConfigReloaded`, `SbieDll_InjectLow_InitSyscalls(TRUE)`, `DriverAssist::RestartHostInjectedSvcs`, `RestartGeneration`, `RestartWorkerActive`, `m_critSecHostInjectedSvcs`, `HostInjectProcessUtil.cpp` `BuildSvcSet`, `g_setSvcNames`, `IsSvcInjected`, `RestartService`, `OpenSCManagerW`, `EnumServicesStatusExW`, `OpenService`, `ControlService`, and `StartServiceW`. |
| Schema | `DRIVERASSIST_HOST_INJECT_RESTART_COALESCING` says each config update increments a generation counter; only one restart worker may be active; the worker waits for a 250ms quiet window before entering SCM work; the worker must not hold `m_critSecHostInjectedSvcs` while sleeping; SCM enumeration/stop/start runs under `m_critSecHostInjectedSvcs`; if a new generation appears while SCM work is running, a worker must run another quiet-window pass; if another worker claims the new generation, the current worker may exit; HostInjectProcess matching and service restart policy must not change. |
| Topology | Every config-update request increments `RestartGeneration`. The first thread that changes `RestartWorkerActive` from 0 to 1 becomes the active worker. It repeatedly samples `RestartGeneration`, sleeps 250ms, and retries until no new generation appears during the sleep interval. It then enters `m_critSecHostInjectedSvcs`, calls `::RestartHostInjectedSvcs()`, leaves the critical section, releases `RestartWorkerActive`, and either exits if no newer generation exists or reclaims/defers worker ownership for the next quiet-window pass. The global restart job still builds the HostInjectProcess set, enumerates active Win32 services, skips `SBIESVC`, compares service names, probes `SbieDll.dll` injection state, and stops/starts only services whose injection state mismatches policy. |
| Logic Risk | The old counter made the expensive SCM job race against reload bursts. It could run based on a transient count rather than a stable post-burst config state. It also described a "waits until the last one" behavior that the implementation did not actually provide. Because service control work is serial inside SCM and can block, repeated incidental restarts are a correctness and availability risk for host-injected services. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-107-driverassist-host-inject-restart-coalescing.md` records Microsoft `EnumServicesStatusExW`, `OpenSCManagerW`, `OpenServiceW`, `ControlService`, `StartServiceW`, and `Sleep` references. `docs/plan/srev-107-driverassist-host-inject-restart-coalescing.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `DRIVERASSIST_HOST_INJECT_RESTART_COALESCING` contract. |
| Fix | `DriverAssist::RestartHostInjectedSvcs` now uses `RestartGeneration` and `RestartWorkerActive`. Every request increments the generation; a single active worker waits until the generation is unchanged across a 250ms interval, then runs `::RestartHostInjectedSvcs()` under `m_critSecHostInjectedSvcs`. If new config-update requests arrive while SCM work is running, the current worker runs or defers another quiet-window pass instead of dropping the request. No HostInjectProcess config parsing, service matching, module-injection detection, SCM enumeration, service stop/start access mask, or service restart policy changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-107.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, generation/active-worker source topology, 250ms quiet-window loop, critical-section placement around SCM work only, stale workaround wording removal, preservation of `HostInjectProcessUtil.cpp` policy, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-107.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows service matrix with rapid config reload bursts, reload during an active SCM restart pass, host-injected service that lacks `SbieDll`, service that should no longer be injected, `SERVICE_WIN32_SHARE_PROCESS`, service stop timeout / `ERROR_SERVICE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, access-denied service, and observation that exactly the stable post-burst `HostInjectProcess` policy is applied. |

### SREV-108: EpMapper Dynamic Port Scope And Binding Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official RPC endpoint mapper, RPC binding lifetime, SCM service-process, and public ALPC event/debugger shape; needs Windows RPC/ALPC dynamic-port runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/EpMapperServer.cpp` resolves sandboxed dynamic RPC endpoints by configured service name or configured RPC interface id, then calls `API_OPEN_DYNAMIC_PORT` with process id `0` so the driver opens the endpoint through its global dynamic-port list. The uncovered comments proposed per-sandbox or per-process filtering, but the current `API_OPEN_DYNAMIC_PORT_ARGS` schema in `Sandboxie/core/drv/api_defs.h` only carries `port_name`, `process_id`, `port_id`, `filter_num`, and `filter_ids`; no box/sandbox scoped key exists. During official-shape review, Microsoft `RpcMgmtEpEltInqNextW` documentation showed a concrete source bug: a returned `Binding` handle is allocated by the RPC runtime and must be released with `RpcBindingFree`. The old loop converted each binding to a string and freed the string, but did not free the binding handle. |
| Data | `EpMapperServer::EpmapperGetPortNameHandler`, `PipeServer::GetCallerProcessId`, `SbieApi_QueryProcess`, `RpcPortBindingIfId`, `RpcPortBindingSvc`, `OpenSCManager`, `OpenService`, `QueryServiceStatusEx`, `SERVICE_STATUS_PROCESS.dwProcessId`, `API_GET_DYNAMIC_PORT_FROM_PID`, `RpcMgmtEpEltInqBegin`, `RpcMgmtEpEltInqNextW`, `RpcBindingToStringBindingW`, `RpcStringFreeW`, `RpcBindingFree`, `RpcMgmtEpEltInqDone`, `RpcPortFilter`, `API_OPEN_DYNAMIC_PORT`, `Ipc_Api_OpenDynamicPort`, `Ipc_CreateDynamicPort`, `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_Dynamic`, `Ipc_GetRpcMsgId`, and `IPC_DYNAMIC_PORT.FilterIDs`. |
| Schema | `EPMAPPER_DYNAMIC_PORT_SCOPE_AND_BINDING_LIFETIME` says the caller process id maps to a sandbox box before policy lookup; `RpcPortBindingIfId` resolves through local RPC endpoint mapper inquiry; `RpcPortBindingSvc` resolves through SCM service process id and driver ALPC owner lookup; each successful `RpcMgmtEpEltInqNextW` binding handle is freed with `RpcBindingFree`; `RpcBindingToStringBindingW` output is freed with `RpcStringFreeW`; `RpcMgmtEpEltInqDone` deletes the inquiry context; `API_OPEN_DYNAMIC_PORT` currently carries process-id or global scope only; process id `0` is the existing global dynamic-port compatibility path; `RpcPortFilter` message ids are attached to the driver dynamic-port entry; per-sandbox dynamic ports require a driver/API schema extension with a scoped key; per-process or per-sandbox ALPC message filtering must not be pretended in service-only code. |
| Topology | Caller pid -> `SbieApi_QueryProcess` -> box name -> endpoint id. Service-name endpoints flow through SCM `QueryServiceStatusEx(SC_STATUS_PROCESS_INFO)` and driver `API_GET_DYNAMIC_PORT_FROM_PID`. Interface-id endpoints flow through `RpcMgmtEpEltInqBegin` / `RpcMgmtEpEltInqNextW` / `RpcBindingToStringBindingW` / `RpcStringFreeW` / `RpcBindingFree` / `RpcMgmtEpEltInqDone`. Successful resolution loads `RpcPortFilter` config and calls `API_OPEN_DYNAMIC_PORT(port, 0, port_id, filter_count, filter_ids)`. The driver stores `IPC_DYNAMIC_PORT` entries in the global `Ipc_Dynamic_Ports` list keyed by port id/name, optionally adds a per-process open path when `process_id != 0`, and applies filter ids when `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_Dynamic` matches a dynamic-port entry and parses the local RPC message id. |
| Logic Risk | Changing the service call from process id `0` to the resolving process id would break the existing cross-process compatibility case noted by the source comment: one sandboxed process may resolve a dynamic endpoint and another may use it. Keeping process id `0` preserves behavior, but the old TODO wording hid the real missing shape: a scoped dynamic-port key in the driver/API contract. Separately, repeated endpoint inquiries leaked RPC binding handles because the RPC runtime owns each `Binding` returned by `RpcMgmtEpEltInqNextW`. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-108-epmapper-dynamic-port-scope-and-binding-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `RpcMgmtEpEltInqBegin`, `RpcMgmtEpEltInqNextW`, `RpcMgmtEpEltInqDone`, `RpcBindingToStringBindingW`, `RpcBindingFree`, `RpcStringFreeW`, `QueryServiceStatusEx`, `SERVICE_STATUS_PROCESS`, ALPC ETW, and LPC/ALPC debugger references. `docs/plan/srev-108-epmapper-dynamic-port-scope-and-binding-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `EPMAPPER_DYNAMIC_PORT_SCOPE_AND_BINDING_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | The interface-id endpoint inquiry loop now frees every successfully returned binding handle with `RpcBindingFree` after string conversion and string cleanup, while preserving the first accepted `LRPC-*` endpoint behavior and `RpcMgmtEpEltInqDone` cleanup. The stale per-sandbox/per-process TODO comments were replaced with a source comment naming the actual API/schema boundary. No endpoint config parsing, SCM lookup, global process-id `0` compatibility path, driver dynamic-port list, or message-id filter policy changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-108.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, endpoint mapper inquiry topology, RPC binding/string/inquiry lifetime calls, stale TODO removal, preservation of process id `0` global compatibility, driver API schema evidence that no sandbox key exists, dynamic-port filter topology, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-108.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows matrix with configured `RpcPortBindingIfId`, configured `RpcPortBindingSvc`, Chrome/Game Config Store style cross-process endpoint use, `RpcPortFilter` allow/deny observations, repeated endpoint lookup under service memory/resource tracing, sandboxed same-box cross-process access, different-box negative case after any future scoped schema change, and ALPC ETW / debugger observation for the actual local-RPC send/receive path. |

### SREV-109: MountManager ImBox State Boundaries

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | source-level classified after official mounted-folder, reparse-point, DOS-device, logical-drive, and volume mount-point enumeration shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/MountManager.cpp` owns ImBox-backed encrypted image / ramdisk mount state. The uncovered comments named mounted-disk discovery, unsupported image update, recovered-device protection query, mounting without a drive letter, and automatic `BoxPassword` use. Microsoft documents mounted folders as volume-to-directory associations, reparse points as tagged caller data with strict buffer/tag/empty-directory rules, `DefineDosDeviceW` as MS-DOS device namespace mapping, and mounted-folder enumeration as volume-scoped. Local source shows the missing state is Sandboxie-specific: `m_RootMap` requires `reg_root` and `file_root`, `BOX_MOUNT::Protected` is set only after `API_PROTECT_ROOT`, `FindImDisk` recovers only `NtPath` / `ImageFile` by ImBox proxy name, `MountImDisk` formats through a temporary drive letter and removes it when not requested, and passwords are request-supplied rather than read from durable config. |
| Data | `MountManager::MountHandler`, `MountManager::AcquireBoxRoot`, `MountManager::FindImDisk`, `MountManager::MountImDisk`, `MountManager::UpdateHandler`, `CreateJunction`, `RemoveJunction`, `BOX_MOUNT`, `BOX_ROOT`, `m_RootMap`, `m_RamDisk`, `IMBOX_MOUNT_REQ`, `IMBOX_UPDATE_REQ`, `SbieDll_Mount`, `req->password`, `API_PROTECT_ROOT`, `API_UNPROTECT_ROOT`, `ImDiskGetDeviceListEx`, `ImDiskQueryDeviceProxy`, `ImDiskFindFreeDriveLetter`, `GetLogicalDrives`, `DefineDosDevice`, `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT`, `FSCTL_DELETE_REPARSE_POINT`, and `BoxPassword`. |
| Schema | `MOUNTMANAGER_IMBOX_STATE_BOUNDARIES` says the constructor does not rebuild `m_RootMap` from devices alone; `FindImDisk` recovers only ImDisk device identity by proxy name; `reg_root` and `file_root` ownership comes from `IMBOX_MOUNT` or `AcquireBoxRoot` requests; `BOX_MOUNT::Protected` becomes true only after `API_PROTECT_ROOT` succeeds; recovered ImDisk devices do not imply protected-root state; `IMBOX_UPDATE` remains unsupported until resize/password rotation has a transaction contract; `MountImDisk` requires a temporary drive-letter path for initial format; unrequested drive-letter mappings are removed after device discovery; `BoxPassword` is not read from durable config in `AcquireBoxRoot`; encrypted image mounts require caller-supplied password until secure handoff exists. |
| Topology | Explicit mounts flow from `SbieDll_Mount` / `IMBOX_MOUNT_REQ` to `MountHandler`, then `FindImDisk` or `MountImDisk`, optional `API_PROTECT_ROOT`, `m_RootMap[reg_root]`, and `CreateJunction(nt_path\\Sandbox, file_root)`. Automatic acquisition flows from `AcquireBoxRoot` through `UseRamDisk` / `UseFileImage`, certificate option checks, `FindImDisk` or `MountImDisk`, and the same root/junction path. `MountImDisk` chooses a requested or free drive letter, launches `ImBox.exe` with `mount=<drive>` and `format=ntfs`, passes password through locked remote memory, reads the returned `\\Device\\ImDiskN`, and removes the DOS-device mapping with `DefineDosDevice(... DDD_REMOVE_DEFINITION | DDD_EXACT_MATCH_ON_REMOVE | DDD_RAW_TARGET_PATH ...)` when the drive letter was temporary. |
| Logic Risk | Treating these TODOs as independent local chores would create false state. Rebuilding roots from devices alone loses registry-root ownership and protection policy. A driver-side protected-state query would need a scoped reg-root/device contract. "Mount without mount" conflicts with the current ImBox/fmifs formatting path. Reading `BoxPassword` from durable config would weaken the explicit credential handoff and needs a separate secure storage design. `IMBOX_UPDATE` declares wire fields but lacks a resize/password-rotation transaction that coordinates ImBox state, image data, protection state, and failure rollback. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-109-mountmanager-imbox-state-boundaries.md` records Microsoft mounted-folder, reparse-point, `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT`, `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT`, `FSCTL_DELETE_REPARSE_POINT`, `DeviceIoControl`, `GetLogicalDrives`, `DefineDosDeviceW`, and mounted-folder enumeration references. `docs/plan/srev-109-mountmanager-imbox-state-boundaries.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `MOUNTMANAGER_IMBOX_STATE_BOUNDARIES` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: constructor recovery is lazy through `FindImDisk` and cannot rebuild `m_RootMap`; `IMBOX_UPDATE` remains unsupported until a transaction/protection refresh contract exists; recovered devices do not restore `Protected` without explicit `API_PROTECT_ROOT`; temporary drive-letter use is required by the current ImBox format path; and `AcquireBoxRoot` does not read durable `BoxPassword` before a secure credential handoff exists. No mount, unmount, ImDisk enumeration, `DefineDosDevice`, reparse-point, password passing, driver protection, or wire-message behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-109.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, removal of uncovered TODO comments, MountManager source comments, explicit mount/acquire/recovery topology, temporary drive-letter cleanup, unsupported update behavior, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-109.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows matrix with explicit encrypted image mount, automatic `UseFileImage`, `UseRamDisk`, service restart with existing ImDisk device, `protect_root` true/false, `admin_only` true/false, temporary drive-letter allocation/removal, requested drive-letter preservation, `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT` and `FSCTL_DELETE_REPARSE_POINT` behavior on empty/non-empty file roots, and negative proof that `IMBOX_UPDATE` remains unsupported. |

### SREV-110: Thread Impersonation Offset Boundary

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | source-level classified after official `PsImpersonateClient`, `PsReferenceImpersonationToken`, `PsRevertToSelf`, impersonation-level, and `SeImpersonatePrivilege` policy shape; comment-only source clarification, no behavior change |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/thread.c` is the top-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after uncovered comment risks reached zero. Its highest-risk path captures an active thread impersonation token with `PsReferenceImpersonationToken`, stores it in `THREAD::token_object`, and later replays it during syscall dispatch through `Thread_MyImpersonateClient`. Microsoft documents that `PsImpersonateClient` may limit impersonation when token SID/restriction conditions are not met, warns that raising an untrusted user thread's privilege state is extremely unsafe, and documents `SecurityIdentification` as identity-only while `SecurityImpersonation` can impersonate locally. Local source intentionally calls `PsImpersonateClient` at `SecurityIdentification`, then updates the private `ETHREAD` client-security field using `Dyndata_Config.ImpersonationData_offset` after verifying the field still points at `TokenObject`; mismatch fails with `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` and logs `MSG_1222`/`0x62`. |
| Data | `Thread_SetThreadToken`, `Thread_StoreThreadToken`, `Thread_MyImpersonateClient`, `Thread_ClearThreadToken`, `PsReferenceImpersonationToken`, `PsImpersonateClient`, `PsGetCurrentThread`, `PsImpersonateClient(..., NULL, ...)`, `THREAD::token_object`, `token_CopyOnOpen`, `token_EffectiveOnly`, `token_ImpersonationLevel`, `Dyndata_Config.ImpersonationData_offset`, XP `PS_IMPERSONATION_INFORMATION`, Vista+ packed client-security pointer/level bits, `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED`, `STATUS_UNKNOWN_REVISION`, `MSG_1222`, and `Process_SetTerminated`. |
| Schema | `THREAD_IMPERSONATION_OFFSET_BOUNDARY` says `Thread_StoreThreadToken` references the active impersonation token before storage; stored thread tokens keep `CopyOnOpen`, `EffectiveOnly`, and local `SecurityImpersonation` replay level; `Thread_SetThreadToken` replays a stored thread token or process primary token for syscall dispatch; `Thread_MyImpersonateClient` calls `PsImpersonateClient` at `SecurityIdentification` first; the `ETHREAD` client-security field update is private dynamic-data owned state; Vista+ validates the masked client-security pointer against `TokenObject` before writing level bits; XP validates `PS_IMPERSONATION_INFORMATION.TokenObject` before writing `ImpersonationLevel`; offset mismatch returns `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED` and logs `MSG_1222`/`0x62`; this source-local SREV must not replace the shim with direct `SecurityImpersonation` or worker-thread dispatch. |
| Topology | Capture path: `PsReferenceImpersonationToken(PsGetCurrentThread)` returns a referenced token plus `CopyOnOpen`, `EffectiveOnly`, and level; under `proc->threads_lock`, Sandboxie stores the token and flags in the thread map and dereferences the replaced token after lock release. Replay path: `Thread_SetThreadToken` reads the stored token under `proc->threads_lock`, references it if present, otherwise uses `proc->primary_token`, calls `Thread_MyImpersonateClient`, and dereferences the transient stored-token reference. Private offset path: `Thread_MyImpersonateClient` calls `PsImpersonateClient(..., SecurityIdentification)`, gets `Dyndata_Config.ImpersonationData_offset`, verifies the XP or Vista+ client-security field points to `TokenObject`, writes only the impersonation level field/bits, and fails closed on mismatch. |
| Logic Risk | This is a compatibility shim, not a public DDI contract. A direct `PsImpersonateClient(..., SecurityImpersonation)` replacement would likely restore the official downgrade behavior that prevents Sandboxie syscalls from running under the original token context. A worker-thread replacement may be architecturally cleaner for privileged work, but it would cross syscall dispatch, token replay, and object filtering ownership. The source-local invariant is therefore fail-closed private-offset verification, not pretending the private offset is generally safe. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-110-thread-impersonation-offset-boundary.md` records Microsoft `PsImpersonateClient`, `PsReferenceImpersonationToken`, `PsRevertToSelf`, `SECURITY_IMPERSONATION_LEVEL`, and `SeImpersonatePrivilege` references. `docs/plan/srev-110-thread-impersonation-offset-boundary.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `THREAD_IMPERSONATION_OFFSET_BOUNDARY` contract. |
| Fix | Comment-only source clarification: the old workaround wording now identifies the path as a Sandboxie compatibility shim, states that the supported API can downgrade the sandboxed caller to `SecurityIdentification`, names the dynamic-data-owned `ETHREAD` client-security update, and states that unverifiable offsets fail closed. No token capture, reference counting, impersonation call, private offset, failure status, process termination, object access filter, or syscall behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-110.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, token capture/replay topology, private offset verification for XP and Vista+ paths, failure status/logging, source comment clarification, absence of a direct `SecurityImpersonation` `PsImpersonateClient` replacement, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-110.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows matrix over each supported build dynamic-data offset, thread token capture through `NtImpersonateAnonymousToken` / `NtImpersonateClientOfPort`, syscall dispatch requiring original token context, offset mismatch fail-closed observation, Driver Verifier, HVCI where supported, and object access filter behavior before and after `Thread_SetThreadToken`. |

### SREV-111: Driver Public Security And Private Handles

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official Windows driver security descriptor, ACL, MIC, private handle, `ZwOpenKey`, `ZwCreateFile`, and `ObReferenceObjectByHandle` shape; needs Windows driver-load / verifier runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/driver.c` is the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-110. Its driver-wide public security setup builds `Driver_PublicAcl`, `Driver_PublicSd`, and `Driver_LowLabelSd`, then other core paths consume those descriptors for file, IPC, key, and token-default-DACL operations. The old source ignored all `NTSTATUS` returns from `RtlCreateAcl`, `RtlAddAccessAllowedAceEx`, `RtlAddAce`, `RtlCreateSecurityDescriptor`, `RtlSetDaclSecurityDescriptor`, and `RtlSetSaclSecurityDescriptor`, so initialization could continue with partially built security descriptors. `Driver_FindHomePath` also opened private registry and file handles without `OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE` before referencing the file handle with `ObReferenceObjectByHandle(..., KernelMode, ...)`. Microsoft documents ACL/security-descriptor DDIs as status-returning, Mandatory Integrity Control labels as SACL `SYSTEM_MANDATORY_LABEL_ACE` entries, private driver handles as `OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE`, and `ObReferenceObjectByHandle(KernelMode)` as expecting a kernel-space handle. |
| Data | `Driver_InitPublicSecurity`, `Driver_PublicAcl`, `Driver_PublicSd`, `Driver_LowLabelSd`, Authenticated Users SID, Everyone SID, Restricted Code SID, `SYSTEM_MANDATORY_LABEL_ACE_TYPE`, `SYSTEM_MANDATORY_LABEL_NO_WRITE_UP`, `SECURITY_MANDATORY_LOW_RID`, `RtlCreateAcl`, `RtlAddAccessAllowedAceEx`, `RtlAddAce`, `RtlCreateSecurityDescriptor`, `RtlSetDaclSecurityDescriptor`, `RtlSetSaclSecurityDescriptor`, `Driver_FindHomePath`, `RegistryPath`, `ImagePath`, `ZwOpenKey`, `ZwQueryValueKey`, `ZwCreateFile`, `FILE_DIRECTORY_FILE`, `OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE`, `ObReferenceObjectByHandle`, and `Obj_GetName`. |
| Schema | `DRIVER_PUBLIC_SECURITY_AND_PRIVATE_HANDLES` says `Driver_InitPublicSecurity` builds absolute security descriptors owned by the driver; ACL and security descriptor construction DDIs return `NTSTATUS` and must fail closed; `Driver_PublicAcl` keeps the existing compatibility DACL for Authenticated Users and Everyone; `Driver_LowLabelSd` keeps the Vista+ low-integrity SACL plus restricted-code DACL path; Mandatory Integrity Control stores an integrity label in an object's SACL through `SYSTEM_MANDATORY_LABEL_ACE`; `Driver_FindHomePath` creates only private driver handles for the registry key and installation directory; private registry/file handles are created with `OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE`; `ObReferenceObjectByHandle(KernelMode)` is used only on the kernel-private file handle; SID values, access masks, descriptor consumers, and public object policy must not change. |
| Topology | Driver load calls `Driver_InitPublicSecurity`, which creates `Driver_PublicAcl`, assigns it to `Driver_PublicSd`, and on Vista+ builds `Driver_LowLabelSd` with a low mandatory-label SACL and Auth/World/Restricted DACL. Later consumers apply `Driver_PublicSd` to created file/IPC objects, apply `Driver_PublicAcl` as a token default DACL, and apply `Driver_LowLabelSd` through `ZwSetSecurityObject(LABEL_SECURITY_INFORMATION)`. Home-path discovery flows from `RegistryPath` to `ZwOpenKey(OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE)`, `ZwQueryValueKey(ImagePath)`, canonicalized installation directory path, `ZwCreateFile(OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE, FILE_DIRECTORY_FILE)`, `ObReferenceObjectByHandle(KernelMode)`, `Obj_GetName`, `ObDereferenceObject`, and `ZwClose`. |
| Logic Risk | A public or low-label descriptor that silently fails during construction undermines every downstream object that assumes that descriptor is valid. Because this is driver initialization, the correct local policy is fail-closed initialization rather than partial compatibility. Separately, missing `OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE` is a handle-boundary smell even when DriverEntry normally runs in a system context; the correct contract is that the registry and file handles are private driver handles, and the later `KernelMode` object reference should only see a kernel-space handle. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-111-driver-public-security-and-private-handles.md` records Microsoft Windows driver security model, SDDL for device objects, `RtlCreateAcl`, `RtlAddAccessAllowedAceEx`, `RtlCreateSecurityDescriptor`, `RtlSetSaclSecurityDescriptor`, Mandatory Integrity Control, Object Handles, `ZwOpenKey`, `ZwCreateFile`, and `ObReferenceObjectByHandle` references. `docs/plan/srev-111-driver-public-security-and-private-handles.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `DRIVER_PUBLIC_SECURITY_AND_PRIVATE_HANDLES` contract. |
| Fix | `Driver_InitPublicSecurity` now checks each ACL/security-descriptor construction `NTSTATUS` and returns `FALSE` on failure through the existing driver-entry failure path. `Driver_FindHomePath` now sets `OBJ_KERNEL_HANDLE` for the private registry key and installation-directory file handles. No SID bytes, access masks, descriptor storage, descriptor consumers, low-integrity label policy, service registry value parsing, canonical path lookup, or file-object reference behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-111.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, checked security-construction source shape, preservation of public/low-label descriptor nodes, private handle topology, stale non-kernel handle patterns, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-111.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows driver-load matrix with Driver Verifier enabled, allocation/failure injection for ACL/security descriptor construction, home-path registry/file open path during normal boot and service restart, low-integrity sandbox path creation, registry low-label application, and public descriptor consumers in file, IPC, token default-DACL, and key paths. |

### SREV-112: ComServer Slave IPC Open Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official named event, mutex, file mapping, mapped-view, handle close, COM initialization, and COM security shape; needs Windows COM proxy runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/comserver.cpp` is the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-111. Its `ComServer::RunSlave` opens parent-created IPC objects by name, maps the shared request/reply area, initializes COM, dispatches requests, and signals the parent reply event. The old source opened `Event2` into `hEvent2` but checked `hEvent1` again; if the reply event open failed while the request event succeeded, the slave entered the COM loop and later called `SetEvent(hEvent2)` with a NULL handle, leaving the parent blocked on the reply event. The same startup path returned directly after opening handles or mapping a view, and suffix replacement assumed the proxy command line contained a colon separator. Microsoft documents `OpenEvent` / `OpenFileMapping` as returning handles that should be closed, mapped views as requiring `UnmapViewOfFile`, file mappings as requiring view unmap plus handle close, and `CoInitializeEx` as required before COM library calls with successful calls balanced by `CoUninitialize`. |
| Data | `ComServer::RunSlave`, `cmdline`, `Global\\`, `Mutex`, `Event1`, `Event2`, `Map`, `OpenMutex`, `OpenEvent`, `OpenFileMapping`, `MapViewOfFile`, `COM_SLAVE_MAP`, `m_heap`, `HeapCreate`, `HeapDestroy`, `CoInitializeEx`, `CoInitializeSecurity`, `CoUninitialize`, `WaitForMultipleObjects`, `SetEvent(hEvent2)`, and parent-side `LockSlave` `CreateMutex` / `CreateEvent` / `CreateFileMapping` / `CreateProcessAsUser`. |
| Schema | `COMSERVER_SLAVE_IPC_OPEN_LIFETIME` says `RunSlave` accepts only a bounded parent-generated proxy command line; the command line must contain the colon separator before object-name suffix replacement; the slave opens the parent-created mutex, request event, reply event, and file mapping by name in the Global namespace; `Event1` is the request event waited by the slave; `Event2` is the reply event signaled by the slave after writing `pMap`; each opened kernel handle is closed on startup failure; each mapped view is unmapped on startup failure; `CoInitializeEx` must succeed before COM library calls; successful `CoInitializeEx` is balanced by `CoUninitialize` on the local cleanup path; object names, access masks, COM request dispatch, and parent-death `ExitProcess` behavior must not change. |
| Topology | Parent service creates named `Mutex`, `Event1`, `Event2`, and `Map` objects and launches `SbieSvc.exe` with the same proxy command-line stem. The slave validates the command-line length and colon separator, opens `Global\\...Mutex`, `Global\\...Event1`, `Global\\...Event2`, and `Global\\...Map`, maps `COM_SLAVE_MAP`, initializes heap and COM STA state, then waits on parent mutex or `Event1`. A request flows from `pMap->msgid` through the corresponding COM slave dispatcher, writes `pMap->exc` / `pMap->hr`, and signals `Event2`. Startup failures flow through one cleanup block that uninitializes COM when initialized, destroys the heap, unmaps the view, and closes opened handles. |
| Logic Risk | `Event1` and `Event2` are opposite edges in the request/reply protocol. Checking the wrong handle after opening `Event2` transforms a failed reply channel into a parent-side hang. Returning directly from partial startup also hid ownership edges for named handles and mapped views. Finally, treating the colon separator as implicit made malformed command-line data a null-pointer write path before any object open failed cleanly. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-112-comserver-slave-ipc-open-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `OpenEventW`, `OpenFileMappingW`, `MapViewOfFile`, file-mapping close, `CloseHandle`, `UnmapViewOfFile`, `CoInitializeEx`, `CoInitializeSecurity`, and mutex object references. `docs/plan/srev-112-comserver-slave-ipc-open-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `COMSERVER_SLAVE_IPC_OPEN_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | `RunSlave` now rejects a malformed command line without a colon, checks `hEvent2` after opening the reply event, routes startup failures to a cleanup block, unmaps mapped views, closes opened mutex/event/file-mapping handles, checks `CoInitializeEx`, and balances successful local COM initialization with `CoUninitialize` on cleanup. No parent object creation, object naming, access masks, COM request ids, shared-map layout, dispatch handlers, `CoInitializeSecurity` arguments, or normal parent-death `ExitProcess` behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-112.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, `RunSlave` colon validation, Event1/Event2 request-reply topology, corrected Event2 failure check, mapped-view/handle cleanup, checked `CoInitializeEx`, preserved dispatch/ExitProcess path, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-112.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows COM proxy startup with normal IPC objects, negative startup matrix for missing Mutex/Event1/Event2/Map, parent wait observation proving `Event2` is signaled after successful dispatch, COM apartment initialization failure injection, and resource observation for mapped-view and handle cleanup on startup failure. |

### SREV-113: Service Entry Resource Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official SCM service entry, command-line, critical-section, and event-log handle lifetime shape; needs Windows SCM/proxy/event-log runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/main.cpp` is the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-112. Its `WinMain` initializes process-wide module state and `DriverAssist::InitializeSidCache`, then either dispatches proxy worker modes from the process command line or connects to the SCM with `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher`. The old source returned directly after each proxy worker returned and returned directly on `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher` failure, skipping `DriverAssist::DestroySidCache`. `InitializeEventLog` also opened a global event-log handle used by `LogEvent`, but service initialization failure after the open and STOP/SHUTDOWN cleanup did not close it. Microsoft documents `GetCommandLineW` as returning the process command-line string, `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher` as the SCM service-process boundary that returns failure through `GetLastError`, critical sections as requiring initialization before use and `DeleteCriticalSection` after initialization, and `OpenEventLog` handles as closed with `CloseEventLog`. |
| Data | `WinMain`, `_Ntdll`, `_Kernel32`, `_SystemInfo`, `DriverAssist::InitializeSidCache`, `DriverAssist::DestroySidCache`, `GetCommandLine`, `SANDBOXIE_ComProxy`, `SANDBOXIE_UacProxy`, `SANDBOXIE_NetProxy`, `SANDBOXIE_GuiProxy`, `SANDBOXIE_UserProxy`, `ComServer::RunSlave`, `ServiceServer::RunUacSlave`, `NetApiServer::RunSlave`, `GuiServer::RunSlave`, `UserServer::RunWorker`, `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher`, `ServiceMain`, `InitializeEventLog`, `OpenEventLog`, `EventLog`, `LogEvent`, `CloseEventLog`, `ServiceHandlerEx`, STOP/SHUTDOWN controls, `DriverAssist::Shutdown`, `MountManager::Shutdown`, and `SetServiceStatus`. |
| Schema | `SERVICE_ENTRY_RESOURCE_LIFETIME` says `WinMain` owns process-wide initialization before the service dispatcher or proxy worker path; `DriverAssist::InitializeSidCache` initializes a process-local critical section before service and proxy paths; every local return path from `WinMain` after SID cache initialization calls `DriverAssist::DestroySidCache`; proxy command-line detection order and proxy worker dispatch targets are unchanged; `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher` remains the SCM boundary for the normal service path; failed `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher` preserves `GetLastError` after SID cache cleanup; `InitializeEventLog` owns the event-log handle opened by `OpenEventLog`; opened event-log handles are closed with `CloseEventLog` on service initialization failure and STOP/SHUTDOWN cleanup; SCM status transitions, accepted controls, and proxy routing must not change. |
