| Topology | Process entry flows from `WinMain` to process-wide module/system-info caching, `DriverAssist::InitializeSidCache`, optional command-line proxy routing, `finish`, `DriverAssist::DestroySidCache`, and return. Normal service startup flows through `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher`, `ServiceMain`, `RegisterServiceCtrlHandlerEx`, `SetServiceStatus(START_PENDING)`, `InitializeEventLog`, `DriverAssist::Initialize`, `InitializePipe`, and `SetServiceStatus(RUNNING)`. Initialization failure after event-log open closes `EventLog`, clears the global handle, and reports STOPPED. STOP/SHUTDOWN flows through `ServiceHandlerEx`, server/driver/mount cleanup, `CloseEventLog`, global handle clear, and `SetServiceStatus(STOPPED)`. |
| Logic Risk | Process-wide resource ownership was split across proxy and SCM topologies. The old source only cleaned the SID cache after successful dispatcher return, leaving proxy-return and dispatcher-failure paths without the matching critical-section cleanup edge. Event-log ownership had a similar missing edge: a successful `OpenEventLog` handle survived initialization failure and service stop cleanup. These are local lifecycle bugs, not a reason to alter proxy command-line markers, SCM table shape, service status values, accepted controls, server startup order, or log formatting. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-113-service-entry-resource-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `GetCommandLineW`, `StartServiceCtrlDispatcherW`, ServiceMain, `RegisterServiceCtrlHandlerExW`, `SetServiceStatus`, critical section objects, `OpenEventLogW`, and `CloseEventLog` references. `docs/plan/srev-113-service-entry-resource-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `SERVICE_ENTRY_RESOURCE_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | `WinMain` now routes all local proxy-return paths and `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher` failure through a shared cleanup label after SID cache initialization. Proxy returns still return `NO_ERROR`; dispatcher failure still returns `GetLastError`; successful dispatcher completion still returns `NO_ERROR`. `ServiceMain` closes and clears `EventLog` if initialization fails after opening it. `ServiceHandlerEx` closes and clears `EventLog` during STOP/SHUTDOWN cleanup. No proxy marker matching, proxy dispatch targets, service table entries, `RegisterServiceCtrlHandlerEx`, `SetServiceStatus` state values, server startup, driver shutdown, mount-manager shutdown, or `LogEvent` formatting changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-113.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, `WinMain` SID cache cleanup topology, preservation of proxy routing and dispatcher error return, event-log handle cleanup on initialization failure and STOP/SHUTDOWN, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-113.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows SCM start/stop matrix, Com/UAC/Net/GUI/User proxy launch matrix, console/debug `StartServiceCtrlDispatcher` failure path, initialization failure after `OpenEventLog`, and event-log handle observation during STOP/SHUTDOWN and service restart. |

### SREV-114: SCM Service Key Path Shape

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official SCM service-name, service-handle, service-registry-key, object-attributes, `NtOpenKey`, and `NtCreateKey` shape; needs Windows SCM/name-length runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/scm.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-113. Its `Scm_OpenServiceWImpl` accepts a service name, invalidates registry merge cache for the Services root and service subkey, opens boxed services through `Scm_OpenKeyForService`, queries host services through SbieSvc, then creates a local pseudo-`SC_HANDLE` with a `tzuk` marker followed by the service-name payload. The old source built `\REGISTRY\MACHINE\SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\<ServiceName>` in a fixed 128-WCHAR stack buffer in `Scm_OpenKeyForService`, and built the same path in a fixed 256-WCHAR temp buffer in `Scm_DiscardKeyCache`. Microsoft documents `OpenServiceW` service names as up to 256 characters, case-insensitive, and not display names; `CreateServiceA` documents the service key created under `HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\System\CurrentControlSet\Services`; `InitializeObjectAttributes` takes a Unicode object name for open/create routines; and `ZwOpenKey` / `ZwCreateKey` consume an `OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES` path and return handles. |
| Data | `Scm_OpenServiceWImpl`, `lpServiceName`, `SCM_SERVICE_NAME_MAX_CHARS`, `Scm_DiscardKeyCache`, `Scm_IsBoxedService`, `Scm_OpenKeyForService`, `Scm_QueryServiceByName`, local `SC_HANDLE` payload, `tzuk`, `Scm_AllocServiceKeyPath`, `Scm_ServicesKeyPath`, `Key_UpdateMergeByPath`, `RtlInitUnicodeString`, `InitializeObjectAttributes`, `NtOpenKey`, `NtCreateKey`, `KEY_QUERY_VALUE`, `KEY_ALL_ACCESS`, `ERROR_INVALID_NAME`, `ERROR_NOT_ENOUGH_MEMORY`, and `RtlNtStatusToDosError`. |
| Schema | `SCM_SERVICE_KEY_PATH_SHAPE` says `OpenServiceW` service names are non-empty and at most 256 characters; Sandboxie local service handles store a `tzuk` marker followed by the service-name payload; lower-casing applies only to the service-name payload and not to the marker; service registry paths are composed from `Scm_ServicesKeyPath`, slash, and service name; service registry path buffers are sized from actual base path and service name length; `Scm_OpenKeyForService` owns the temporary path buffer until `NtOpenKey` or `NtCreateKey` returns; `Scm_DiscardKeyCache` invalidates Services root and service subkey while preserving caller last error; access masks, boxed-service lookup, SbieSvc query routing, key-cache invalidation targets, and handle marker layout must not change. |
| Topology | `Scm_OpenServiceWImpl` validates `HANDLE_SERVICE_MANAGER`, validates non-empty service name, rejects names longer than `SCM_SERVICE_NAME_MAX_CHARS`, calls `Scm_DiscardKeyCache`, chooses boxed-service registry lookup or SbieSvc host query, allocates a local pseudo-handle, writes the `tzuk` marker, copies the service-name payload, and lower-cases only that payload. `Scm_AllocServiceKeyPath` owns the `Services\ServiceName` string composition. `Scm_OpenKeyForService` uses the helper path with `RtlInitUnicodeString`, `InitializeObjectAttributes`, `NtOpenKey` or `NtCreateKey`, maps status to Win32 last-error values, and frees the temporary path. `Scm_DiscardKeyCache` updates the Services root merge cache, then updates the service subkey merge cache when a legal path can be built, and restores the incoming last-error value. |
| Logic Risk | The old fixed buffers were shorter than the legal upstream data shape once the service-registry base path and separator were included. That turns a valid or near-valid SCM service name into local memory corruption before `NtOpenKey` / `NtCreateKey` can return a normal error. The old `_wcslwr(name)` also treated marker-plus-payload memory as one wide string; the local handle schema is marker first, string second, so string transforms must start at the payload pointer. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-114-scm-service-key-path-shape.md` records Microsoft `OpenServiceW`, `CreateServiceA`, `CloseServiceHandle`, `InitializeObjectAttributes`, `ZwOpenKey`, `ZwCreateKey`, and registry element size-limit references. `docs/plan/srev-114-scm-service-key-path-shape.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `SCM_SERVICE_KEY_PATH_SHAPE` contract. |
| Fix | `Scm_OpenServiceWImpl` now rejects service names longer than `SCM_SERVICE_NAME_MAX_CHARS`, checks the local handle allocation, and lower-cases only the service-name payload after the marker. New `Scm_AllocServiceKeyPath` centralizes length-derived service registry path allocation. `Scm_OpenKeyForService` and `Scm_DiscardKeyCache` use that helper instead of fixed stack/temp buffers. `Scm_DiscardKeyCache` preserves the caller-visible last-error value because cache invalidation is not the public result of the SCM operation. No access masks, boxed-service classification, SbieSvc query routing, `NtOpenKey` / `NtCreateKey` status mapping, key-cache invalidation targets, or local handle marker layout changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-114.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, service-name length validation, dynamic service-key path helper, `Scm_OpenKeyForService` ownership/free topology, `Scm_DiscardKeyCache` last-error preservation, removal of old fixed buffers, payload-only lower-casing, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-114.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime gate: Windows matrix for `OpenServiceW` / `OpenServiceA` with short, 128-char, 255-char, 256-char, and 257-char names; boxed-service registry-key open with near-limit names; key-cache invalidation observation; invalid slash/backslash/empty/over-limit names; and existing BITS/WUAU/MSIServer/TrustedInstaller/CryptSvc compatibility paths. |

### SREV-115: My WinNT Warning Boundary

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | patched source-level after official MSVC warning pragma and C4267 warning shape; needs Windows WDK build/runtime consumer proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/my_winnt.h` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-114. The file is a driver-wide NT compatibility shim: it includes `ntifs.h` and `alpc.h`, then declares missing or private NT constants, prototypes, object-manager structures, system-information structures, and thread/process helpers used by many driver modules. The old header disabled MSVC warning C4267 at file scope without a matching restore. Microsoft documents C4267 as conversion from `size_t` to a smaller type, and documents `#pragma warning(push)` / `#pragma warning(pop)` as storing and restoring warning state, specifically useful for headers. |
| Data | `my_winnt.h`, include guard `_MY_WINNT_H`, `#pragma warning(disable : 4267)`, `#pragma warning(push)`, `#pragma warning(pop)`, `ntifs.h`, `alpc.h`, `OBJECT_TYPE`, `OBJECT_HEADER`, `SYSTEM_PROCESS_INFORMATION`, `SYSTEM_MODULE_INFORMATION`, `ZwQuerySystemInformation`, process/thread access masks, and private NT compatibility declarations. |
| Schema | `MY_WINNT_WARNING_BOUNDARY` says `my_winnt.h` is a driver-wide NT compatibility shim for declarations not supplied by every target WDK/DDK combination; private NT structure declarations are compatibility data shapes and not architecture truth by themselves; the header may suppress C4267 only for declarations inside the header; any warning-state mutation introduced by the header is restored before the include guard closes; the C4267 suppression does not cross into includer code; private NT structure layouts, function prototypes, access masks, object-manager structures, and system-information structures must not change in this SREV. |
| Topology | Driver source includes `driver.h` / `util.h`, which include `my_winnt.h`. The header includes WDK and local ALPC declarations, pushes MSVC warning state, suppresses C4267 only for the compatibility declarations in the guarded header body, declares private NT shapes, then pops warning state before the include guard closes. Downstream driver code resumes the includer's original warning state. |
| Logic Risk | Header-level warning mutations are global to the remainder of the including translation unit unless explicitly restored. Leaving C4267 disabled past this shim can hide 64-bit truncation mistakes in later driver code. The private NT declarations in this file are also high-risk, but they cannot be made correct by average-pattern cleanup; they require WDK/build/runtime layout validation. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-115-my-winnt-warning-boundary.md` records Microsoft `#pragma warning` and compiler warning C4267 references. `docs/plan/srev-115-my-winnt-warning-boundary.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `MY_WINNT_WARNING_BOUNDARY` contract. |
| Fix | `my_winnt.h` now wraps the C4267 suppression with `#pragma warning(push)` and `#pragma warning(pop)`. No NT declarations, prototypes, access masks, structure layouts, include order, or consumers changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-115.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, header warning push/disable/pop order, one push and one pop, preservation of key private NT declaration nodes, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-115.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows WDK build with normal warning level proving includer warning state is restored after `my_winnt.h`, x86/x64/ARM64 driver compile matrix, runtime smoke for consumers of `SYSTEM_PROCESS_INFORMATION`, `SYSTEM_MODULE_INFORMATION`, object-manager structures, and ALPC declarations, plus Driver Verifier / HVCI where supported. |

### SREV-116: Advapi Header Out-Param Schema

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official Advapi/Cred pointer-depth and output-parameter shape; needs Windows build/runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/advapi.h` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-115. The header owns Advapi/Cred function pointer typedefs consumed by `Sandboxie/core/dll/advapi.c` and `Sandboxie/core/dll/cred.c`. The old `P_GetSecurityInfo` typedef and matching `AdvApi_GetSecurityInfo` / `Ntmarta_GetSecurityInfo` hook prototypes used `PSID` and `PACL` where Microsoft documents `PSID *` and `PACL *` output slots. The old `P_CredRead` used `void *` where Microsoft documents a credential output slot, and `P_CredEnumerate` used `void *` where Microsoft documents a credential-array output slot. `P_LookupAccountName` was also declared twice with the same shape. |
| Data | `P_GetSecurityInfo`, `AdvApi_GetSecurityInfo`, `Ntmarta_GetSecurityInfo`, `P_CredRead`, `P_CredEnumerate`, `P_LookupAccountName`, `__sys_GetSecurityInfo`, `__sys_Ntmarta_GetSecurityInfo`, `__sys_CredReadA/W`, `__sys_CredEnumerateA/W`, `ppsidOwner`, `ppsidGroup`, `ppDacl`, `ppSacl`, `ppCredential`, and `ppCredentials`. |
| Schema | `ADVAPI_HEADER_OUT_PARAM_SCHEMA` says `advapi.h` owns the hooked Advapi/Cred function pointer shapes shared by `advapi.c` and `cred.c`; hook typedefs that model Windows APIs preserve pointer-depth for out parameters; `GetSecurityInfo` receives `PSID *`, `PSID *`, `PACL *`, `PACL *`, and `PSECURITY_DESCRIPTOR *` output slots; `CredRead` receives a credential output slot, not a credential value; `CredEnumerate` receives a credential-array output slot, not an opaque input pointer; hook implementation prototypes in `advapi.c` match the hooked API pointer typedef; this SREV does not change hook selection, access masks, window-station fallback behavior, credential serialization, credential conversion, or PStore merge logic. |
| Topology | Windows Advapi/Cred API contracts flow into `advapi.h` function pointer typedefs, then into resolved `__sys_*` function pointers, `SBIEDLL_HOOK` replacement signatures, and wrapper forwarding calls in `advapi.c` / `cred.c`. Output slots cross that topology unchanged so ownership and writeback edges remain visible at each layer. |
| Logic Risk | Compressing output slots into plain pointer values hides the fact that the API writes back pointers owned by the returned security descriptor or credential block. The common Windows ABI may still pass pointer-sized values successfully, but the local schema becomes false: future hook edits, static checks, or compiler diagnostics cannot tell whether a parameter is data, an output slot, or an array output slot. Duplicate typedefs also make the header's API map less trustworthy during review. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-116-advapi-header-out-param-schema.md` records Microsoft `GetSecurityInfo`, `CredReadW`, `CredEnumerateW`, and `LookupAccountNameW` references. `docs/plan/srev-116-advapi-header-out-param-schema.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `ADVAPI_HEADER_OUT_PARAM_SCHEMA` contract. |
| Fix | `advapi.h` now preserves official pointer-depth for `P_GetSecurityInfo`, `P_CredRead` (`void **ppCredential`), and `P_CredEnumerate` (`void ***ppCredential`), and keeps only one `P_LookupAccountName` typedef. `advapi.c` hook prototypes and forwarding calls now use `ppsidOwner`, `ppsidGroup`, `ppDacl`, and `ppSacl` to match the `GetSecurityInfo` API shape. No hook selection, `SetSecurityInfo` behavior, window-station fallback decision, credential serialization, ANSI/Wide conversion, PStore merge, access mask, or runtime policy was changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-116.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, header typedef pointer-depth, singular `P_LookupAccountName`, `advapi.c` hook prototype and forwarding shape, `cred.c` consumer shape, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-116.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows x86/x64 build proving the typed hooks match real imported API prototypes, runtime smoke for `GetSecurityInfo` / `ntmarta!GetSecurityInfo` DACL fallback on dummy window station, and sandboxed `CredReadA/W` plus `CredEnumerateA/W` with native and PStore-backed credentials. |

### SREV-117: Process Util Allocation Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official pool-allocation, system-thread, handle-close, and string-format shape; needs Windows driver build/fault-injection proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/process_util.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-116. `Process_ScheduleKill` allocated a two-slot context with `Mem_Alloc(Driver_Pool, sizeof(PVOID)*2)`, immediately wrote `params[0]` and `params[1]`, and then passed the context to `PsCreateSystemThread`. The local pool backend can return `NULL`. If allocation failed, the old code dereferenced NULL. If `PsCreateSystemThread` failed after allocation, no worker owned the context and the caller returned `FALSE` without freeing it. `Process_LogMessage` similarly allocated transient log text and then called `RtlStringCbPrintfW` without checking the allocation result. |
| Data | `Process_LogMessage`, `Process_ScheduleKill`, `Process_ScheduleKillProc`, `Mem_Alloc`, `Mem_Free`, `RtlStringCbPrintfW`, `PsCreateSystemThread`, `ZwClose`, `PsTerminateSystemThread`, `Driver_Pool`, `proc->pool`, log text buffer, two-slot kill context, returned system-thread handle, and worker start context. |
| Schema | `PROCESS_UTIL_ALLOCATION_LIFETIME` says `Mem_Alloc` may return `NULL` because its pool backend may fail allocation; any buffer passed to `RtlStringCbPrintfW` must be allocated before use; `Process_LogMessage` may skip logging when transient log-text allocation fails; `Process_ScheduleKill` owns the two-slot thread context until `PsCreateSystemThread` succeeds; after `PsCreateSystemThread` succeeds, `Process_ScheduleKillProc` owns and frees the thread context; if `PsCreateSystemThread` fails, `Process_ScheduleKill` frees the thread context and returns `FALSE`; the returned system-thread handle remains closed by the caller after successful `PsCreateSystemThread`; this SREV does not change termination policy, delay/retry behavior, process handle access masks, service-cancel fallback, log message ids, or worker termination status. |
| Topology | `Process_LogMessage` flows through transient pool allocation, allocation gate, `RtlStringCbPrintfW`, `Log_MsgP1`, and `Mem_Free`. `Process_ScheduleKill` flows through Driver_Pool context allocation, allocation gate, context fill, `PsCreateSystemThread`, caller-side `ZwClose(handle)` on success, worker-side context free in `Process_ScheduleKillProc`, or caller-side context free on thread-create failure. |
| Logic Risk | Termination and logging paths often run when the system is already under pressure. Treating allocation as infallible converts low-memory handling into a kernel crash. Treating `PsCreateSystemThread` as an unconditional ownership transfer leaks the context when thread creation fails. The correct local fix is a lifetime gate, not a policy change to who may be killed, which process access mask is used, or how delayed retry works. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-117-process-util-allocation-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `ExAllocatePoolWithTag`, `PsCreateSystemThread`, `ZwClose`, and `RtlStringCbPrintfW` references. `docs/plan/srev-117-process-util-allocation-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `PROCESS_UTIL_ALLOCATION_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | `Process_LogMessage` now returns without logging if transient log-text allocation fails. `Process_ScheduleKill` now returns `FALSE` if the two-slot worker context cannot be allocated, and frees that context with `Mem_Free(params, sizeof(PVOID)*2)` if `PsCreateSystemThread` fails after allocation. The successful path is unchanged: caller closes the returned system-thread handle, worker frees the context at entry, and normal immediate/delayed kill behavior remains intact. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-117.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, nullable pool allocation shape, `Process_LogMessage` allocation-before-format gate, `Process_ScheduleKill` context allocation gate, caller-side free on `PsCreateSystemThread` failure, worker-side context free on successful transfer, successful thread-handle close, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-117.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows driver build for `process_util.c`, pool allocation failure injection for log and schedule-kill paths, `PsCreateSystemThread` failure injection after context allocation, and normal immediate/delayed kill smoke proving the thread handle is closed and context is freed by the worker. |

### SREV-118: IPC LSA Counted Port Name

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION` / `UNICODE_STRING` and `RtlEqualUnicodeString` shape; needs Windows driver build/object-name runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/ipc_lsa.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-117. `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_Lsa` and `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_LsaEP` receive an `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION *Name`, check `Name->Name.Length`, then compared `Name->Name.Buffer` to literal LSA endpoint names with `_wcsicmp`. Microsoft documents `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION` as containing `UNICODE_STRING Name`; `UNICODE_STRING.Length` is a byte count and does not include a terminating NUL if present. Therefore length checks alone do not make `Name->Name.Buffer` safe for C-string scanning. |
| Data | `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_Lsa`, `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_LsaEP`, `Ipc_Lsa_MatchPortName`, `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION.Name`, `UNICODE_STRING.Length`, `UNICODE_STRING.Buffer`, `RtlInitUnicodeString`, `RtlEqualUnicodeString`, `\LsaAuthenticationPort`, `\RPC Control\lsasspirpc`, `\RPC Control\LSARPC_ENDPOINT`, KPATH-004 LSAD opnum policy, and KPATH-006 RPC payload capture. |
| Schema | `IPC_LSA_COUNTED_PORT_NAME` says `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION.Name` is a counted `UNICODE_STRING`; `UNICODE_STRING.Length` is a byte count and does not prove `Buffer` is NUL-terminated; LSA endpoint matching must compare counted strings, not C-string scans over `Name->Name.Buffer`; `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_Lsa` continues to accept only `\LsaAuthenticationPort` and `\RPC Control\lsasspirpc` when `ipc_block_password` is enabled; `Ipc_CheckPortRequest_LsaEP` continues to accept only `\RPC Control\LSARPC_ENDPOINT` unless `OpenLsaEndpoint` is enabled; this SREV does not change MS-LSAD opnum policy, password-change detection, KPATH-004 secret/private-data denial, KPATH-006 RPC payload capture, or `OpenLsaEndpoint` behavior. |
| Topology | Object manager name data flows as `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION.Name { Length, MaximumLength, Buffer }` into `ipc_lsa.c` port-name gates. `Ipc_Lsa_MatchPortName` initializes the expected literal with `RtlInitUnicodeString`, then compares counted strings using `RtlEqualUnicodeString(..., TRUE)`. Only after the object-name boundary matches does the LSA authentication-port password filter or LSARPC endpoint opnum filter run. |
| Logic Risk | `_wcsicmp` searches for a terminating NUL and may read past the counted object-name buffer. In an endpoint filter, that turns a simple name gate into an out-of-bounds read before the LSARPC policy code even runs. The fix is independent from KPATH-004 and KPATH-006: those gates still own opnum semantics and RPC payload parsing, while this SREV owns the earlier object-name data shape. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-118-ipc-lsa-counted-port-name.md` records Microsoft `ObQueryNameString` / `OBJECT_NAME_INFORMATION`, `UNICODE_STRING`, `RtlEqualUnicodeString`, and `RtlInitUnicodeString` references. `docs/plan/srev-118-ipc-lsa-counted-port-name.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `IPC_LSA_COUNTED_PORT_NAME` contract. |
| Fix | `ipc_lsa.c` now has `Ipc_Lsa_MatchPortName`, which compares `Name->Name` to endpoint literals as counted Unicode strings. The three LSA endpoint gates no longer call `_wcsicmp(Name->Name.Buffer, ...)`. No LSARPC opnum allow/deny decision, RPC message-id extraction, trace capture, password-change scan, event-log message, `OpenLsaEndpoint` escape hatch, or Windows-version branch changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-118.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, counted `UNICODE_STRING` endpoint helper, removal of `_wcsicmp(Name->Name.Buffer, ...)` from `ipc_lsa.c`, preservation of KPATH-004 secret/private-data opnum cases, preservation of KPATH-006 `Ipc_GetRpcMsgId` routing, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-118.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows driver build for `ipc_lsa.c`, object-name instrumentation proving counted endpoint matching works without a trailing NUL, normal LSA authentication-port and LSARPC endpoint traffic, and unchanged KPATH-004/KPATH-006 runtime gates. |

### SREV-119: Low Init Protect Write Gate

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official memory-protection, instruction-cache, and Nt/Zw native-service shape; needs Windows low-level bootstrap runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/low/init.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-118. Its bootstrap path patches local low-level data, syscall export stubs, and ARM64 `LdrQueryImageFileExecutionOptionsEx` CHPE detours by first calling `NtProtectVirtualMemory(..., PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE, &OldProtect)`. The old source did not check the returned `NTSTATUS`; if the protection change failed, it still wrote through the target pointer and later restored with an uninitialized `OldProtect`. Microsoft documents the public `VirtualProtect` shape as changing protection on committed pages, failing without modifying protection when the requested region is invalid, and writing the old protection through an output pointer; Microsoft also documents that modified executable code requires instruction-cache coherency, and documents user-mode native-service calls as validated Nt/Zw system-service calls. |
| Data | `WriteMemorySafe`, `InitSyscalls`, `DisableCHPE`, `NtProtectVirtualMemory`, `NT_SUCCESS`, `PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE`, `OldProtect`, `RegionBase`, `RegionSize`, `SystemService`, `ZwXxxPtr`, `SyscallPtr`, `SyscallNum`, `RtlImageOptionsEx_tramp`, `RtlImageOptionsEx`, `NtFlushInstructionCache`, `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, and `EcExitThunkPtr`. |
| Schema | `LOW_INIT_PROTECT_WRITE_GATE` says `init.c` code or data patches that request `PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE` through `NtProtectVirtualMemory` must check `NTSTATUS` before writing; `OldProtect` is initialized before each writable-protection attempt; `OldProtect` is restored only after the writable-protection call succeeds; `WriteMemorySafe` fails closed when the protection change fails; `InitSyscalls` skips the current syscall export when the target stub cannot be made writable; `DisableCHPE` returns before trampoline or target-detour byte writes when the relevant region cannot be made writable; this SREV does not change syscall selection, detour bytes, ARM64EC wrapper routing, CHPE policy, or instruction-cache flush placement. |
| Topology | Early bootstrap data flows into local code/data patch sites. Each patch site first calls `NtProtectVirtualMemory` for `PAGE_EXECUTE_READWRITE`, checks `NT_SUCCESS(status)`, writes local bytes only after that gate, restores `OldProtect`, and keeps existing `NtFlushInstructionCache` calls for executable code patches. |
| Logic Risk | In low-level process bootstrap, an unchecked protection transition turns a failed precondition into a direct write through an address that may still be non-writable. That can crash the initializing process, skip cleanup, or leave waiting threads stuck behind the init lock. The correct local repair is a fail-closed protection gate, not a new policy decision about which syscall stubs, jump-table bytes, ARM64EC wrapper state, or CHPE behavior should exist. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-119-low-init-protect-write-gate.md` records Microsoft `VirtualProtect`, `FlushInstructionCache`, and Nt/Zw native-service references. `docs/plan/srev-119-low-init-protect-write-gate.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `LOW_INIT_PROTECT_WRITE_GATE` contract. |
| Fix | `WriteMemorySafe`, `InitSyscalls`, and `DisableCHPE` now store the `NtProtectVirtualMemory` return value, initialize `OldProtect`, and write only after `NT_SUCCESS(status)`. `WriteMemorySafe` returns without writing on failure. `InitSyscalls` advances to the next syscall record when the current export cannot be made writable. `DisableCHPE` returns before copying trampoline or target detour bytes when the corresponding region cannot be made writable. No syscall-data layout, hook bytes, jump-table routing, FFS/ARM64EC routing, CHPE disabling policy, restore call, or instruction-cache flush call changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-119.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, local `NtProtectVirtualMemory` / `NT_SUCCESS` typedef shape, checked writable-protection gates in `WriteMemorySafe`, `InitSyscalls`, and `DisableCHPE`, preservation of instruction-cache flush calls, preserved SREV-106 ARM64EC context, stale unchecked local protect-write pattern removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-119.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows x86/x64/ARM64 low-level build for `init.c`, normal sandbox process startup with syscall hooks enabled and disabled, ARM64EC/CHPE startup where available, protection-failure instrumentation proving no write occurs after failed writable-protection transition, and Driver Verifier / loader-lock observation during early process initialization. |

### SREV-120: Terminal User Token Session Contract

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official WTS session-token and handle-duplication shape; needs Windows Terminal Services runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/terminal.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-119. Its `Terminal_WTSQueryUserToken(ULONG SessionId, HANDLE *pToken)` hook accepted the official `SessionId` parameter but sent only a bare `MSG_HEADER` through `Sandboxie/core/svc/terminalwire.h` to `Sandboxie/core/svc/terminalserver.cpp` `TerminalServer::GetUserToken`. The service then derived the caller process session through `SbieApi_QueryProcess` and called `WTSQueryUserToken(session_id, &hToken)`. Microsoft documents `WTSQueryUserToken` as obtaining the primary access token of the logged-on user specified by the session ID, warns that highly trusted services must not leak user tokens, and documents returned token handles as caller-close owned. Microsoft documents `DuplicateHandle` as duplicating a valid source handle into a target process handle that has `PROCESS_DUP_HANDLE`. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/dll/terminal.c`, `Sandboxie/core/svc/terminalwire.h`, `Sandboxie/core/svc/terminalserver.cpp`, `Terminal_WTSQueryUserToken`, `GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ`, `GET_USER_TOKEN_RPL`, `SessionId`, `req->session_id`, `TerminalServer::GetUserToken`, `PipeServer::GetCallerProcessId`, `SbieApi_QueryProcess`, caller session id, `WTSQueryUserToken`, `API_FILTER_TOKEN`, `OriginalToken`, `UnfilteredToken`, `DuplicateHandle`, `PROCESS_DUP_HANDLE`, `TOKEN_ALL_ACCESS`, and `CloseHandle(hToken)`. |
| Schema | `TERMINAL_USER_TOKEN_SESSION_CONTRACT` says `WTSQueryUserToken` is keyed by an explicit Remote Desktop Services `SessionId`; `Terminal_WTSQueryUserToken` must forward the caller requested `SessionId` in `GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ`; `TerminalServer::GetUserToken` validates the full `GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ` size; TerminalServer only satisfies the request when requested session equals the `SbieApi_QueryProcess` caller session; TerminalServer calls `WTSQueryUserToken` with the validated requested session id; token filtering, `OriginalToken`, `UnfilteredToken`, `DuplicateHandle`, and close-handle ownership are unchanged. |
| Topology | A sandboxed process calls `Terminal_WTSQueryUserToken(SessionId, pToken)`, which sends `GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ { MSG_HEADER, session_id }` to TerminalServer. The service resolves the pipe caller pid, queries Sandboxie process state for the caller session, rejects mismatched requested sessions with `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`, calls `WTSQueryUserToken(req->session_id, &hToken)` only after that gate, optionally filters the token through `API_FILTER_TOKEN`, duplicates the resulting token handle into the caller process, closes its local token handle, and returns the duplicated handle in `GET_USER_TOKEN_RPL`. |
| Logic Risk | Dropping `SessionId` at the wire boundary made the hook return a token for the caller-derived session even when the caller asked for a different session. Passing the requested session directly to the trusted service without a caller-session gate would create the opposite bug: a sandboxed caller could ask the LocalSystem broker for another session's user token. The correct local boundary is explicit requested-session data plus same-session authorization before the trusted WTS call. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-120-terminal-user-token-session-contract.md` records Microsoft `WTSQueryUserToken`, `WTSEnumerateSessionsW`, and `DuplicateHandle` references. `docs/plan/srev-120-terminal-user-token-session-contract.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `TERMINAL_USER_TOKEN_SESSION_CONTRACT` contract. |
| Fix | `GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ` now carries `ULONG session_id`. `Terminal_WTSQueryUserToken` writes its `SessionId` argument into that field. `TerminalServer::GetUserToken` now validates `sizeof(GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ)`, rejects requests whose `req->session_id` differs from the caller process session returned by `SbieApi_QueryProcess`, and calls `WTSQueryUserToken(req->session_id, &hToken)` after that gate. No token filtering policy, `OriginalToken` / `UnfilteredToken` configuration, `API_FILTER_TOKEN` routing, `DuplicateHandle` target process, token close path, or reply handle shape changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-120.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, `GET_USER_TOKEN_REQ` wire shape, DLL-side forwarding of the requested session id, service-side full request-size validation, same-session authorization gate, validated `WTSQueryUserToken(req->session_id, ...)` call, preserved token filtering / duplication / close-handle topology, stale bare-header validation removal, stale caller-derived WTS argument removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-120.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows build for `terminal.c`, `terminalwire.h`, and `terminalserver.cpp`; sandboxed `WTSQueryUserToken(Dll_SessionId, ...)` smoke proving the duplicated token handle is usable and caller-closed; negative different-session request smoke returning `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`; `OriginalToken`, `UnfilteredToken`, and filtered-token policy matrix; and 32-bit / 64-bit caller-service handle-duplication matrix. |

### SREV-121: User Server Duplicate Handle Result Gate

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official Win32 handle-duplication, APC enqueue, close-handle, and process-open shape; needs Windows service build/fault-injection proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/UserServer.cpp` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-120. `UserServer::StartWorker` duplicated the service process handle into the worker process with `DuplicateHandle`, but wrapped that Win32 `BOOL` API in `NT_SUCCESS`. Since `FALSE` is zero and `NT_SUCCESS(0)` is true, a failed duplicate could still queue `UserServer__APC` with an uninitialized handle. `UserServer::OpenFile` also ignored the `DuplicateHandle` return value when duplicating a broker-opened file handle into the sandboxed caller, so a duplicate failure could leave `USER_OPEN_FILE_RPL.FileHandle` uninitialized while `rpl->error` still reflected a successful `NtCreateFile`. |
| Data | `UserServer::StartWorker`, `UserServer::OpenFile`, `DuplicateHandle`, `QueueUserAPC`, `OpenProcess`, `CloseHandle`, `NtCurrentProcess`, `pi.hProcess`, `pi.hThread`, `hThis`, `hProcess`, `hFile`, `USER_OPEN_FILE_RPL.FileHandle`, `rpl->error`, `PROCESS_DUP_HANDLE`, and `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL`. |
| Schema | `USER_SERVER_DUPLICATE_HANDLE_RESULT_GATE` says `DuplicateHandle` returns a Win32 `BOOL`, not an `NTSTATUS`; `DuplicateHandle` success is nonzero and failure is zero; `StartWorker` must not use `NT_SUCCESS` around `DuplicateHandle`; `StartWorker` queues `UserServer__APC` only after duplicate success; `StartWorker` closes the duplicate handle if `QueueUserAPC` fails; `OpenFile` initializes `USER_OPEN_FILE_RPL.FileHandle` to zero before duplication; `OpenFile` reports `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL` when duplicating the broker-opened handle into the caller process fails; this SREV does not change user-worker token selection, event security, file-policy path matching, requested access, `NtCreateFile` arguments, or queue routing. |
| Topology | `StartWorker` flows from a broker-owned service-process pseudo handle through `DuplicateHandle` into the worker process, then through `QueueUserAPC` to transfer the duplicated handle to the worker thread; if APC enqueue fails, the broker closes the duplicate it still owns. `OpenFile` flows from broker-side `NtCreateFile`, through `OpenProcess(PROCESS_DUP_HANDLE, caller pid)`, through `DuplicateHandle` into the caller process, then into `USER_OPEN_FILE_RPL.FileHandle`; failed handle crossing is represented by zero `FileHandle` plus `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL`, and the broker still closes its local file handle with `NtClose`. |
| Logic Risk | Treating a Win32 `BOOL` API as an `NTSTATUS` success code inverts the failure gate for zero-valued failure. In the worker-start path that can transfer an indeterminate handle value through APC; in the file-open broker path it can return a success-shaped reply without a valid caller handle. The correct local fix is a result gate at the existing handle boundary, not a policy change to who may start a worker, which file path rules apply, or which requested access flags are honored. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-121-user-server-duplicate-handle-result-gate.md` records Microsoft `DuplicateHandle`, `QueueUserAPC`, `CloseHandle`, and `OpenProcess` references. `docs/plan/srev-121-user-server-duplicate-handle-result-gate.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `USER_SERVER_DUPLICATE_HANDLE_RESULT_GATE` contract. |
| Fix | `StartWorker` now initializes `hThis = NULL`, gates `QueueUserAPC` on nonzero `DuplicateHandle` success, and closes `hThis` if `QueueUserAPC` fails after the duplicate was created. `OpenFile` now initializes `rpl->FileHandle = 0` before `NtCreateFile` and sets `rpl->error = STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL` if `DuplicateHandle` cannot duplicate the broker-opened file handle into the caller process. No user-worker token selection, event security descriptor, process creation flags, file-policy matching, requested file access, EA-buffer handling, `NtCreateFile` argument shape, broker-side `NtClose`, or queue callback routing changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-121.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, removal of `NT_SUCCESS(DuplicateHandle(...))`, worker APC enqueue gated on duplicate success, duplicate-handle cleanup on `QueueUserAPC` failure, `OpenFile` reply-handle zero initialization, duplicate-handle failure reported as `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL`, preserved broker-side `NtClose`, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-121.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows service build for `UserServer.cpp`, worker-start positive smoke proving the APC-transferred synchronization handle is usable, APC failure injection proving the duplicate is closed, sandboxed `USER_OPEN_FILE` positive smoke proving the duplicated handle works, and negative `OpenProcess` / `DuplicateHandle` smoke proving the reply carries `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL` with zero `FileHandle`. |

### SREV-122: Process Force Map Allocation Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official kernel pool-allocation, resource-lock, and IRQL shape; needs Windows driver build/fault-injection proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/process_force.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-121. `Process_DfpInsert` allocated `FORCE_PROCESS_2` records for `Process_MapDfp` and immediately wrote `proc->pid` / `proc->silent`; `Process_FcpInsert` allocated `FORCE_PROCESS_3` records for `Process_MapFcp` and immediately wrote `proc->pid` / `proc->boxname`. The local `Mem_Alloc` backend can return `NULL`, matching the official Windows pool-allocation failure shape. A failed allocation would therefore null-dereference while DFP/FCP process-map insertion was in progress, including self-locked paths that had raised IRQL and acquired `Process_ListLock`. |
| Data | `Process_DfpInsert`, `Process_FcpInsert`, `Process_DfpDelete`, `Process_FcpDelete`, `Process_MapDfp`, `Process_MapFcp`, `FORCE_PROCESS_2`, `FORCE_PROCESS_3`, `Mem_Alloc`, `map_insert`, `Process_ListLock`, `KeRaiseIrql`, `KeLowerIrql`, `ExAcquireResourceExclusiveLite`, `ExReleaseResourceLite`, `PROCESS_TERMINATED`, `pid`, `silent`, and `boxname`. |
| Schema | `PROCESS_FORCE_MAP_ALLOCATION_LIFETIME` says `Mem_Alloc` may return `NULL` because pool allocation can fail; `Process_DfpInsert(PROCESS_TERMINATED, ProcessId)` owns the DFP record allocation while it holds `Process_ListLock`, and allocation failure releases `Process_ListLock`, restores IRQL, and returns `FALSE`; `Process_DfpInsert(ParentId, ProcessId)` is called with the process list already locked, and child allocation failure returns `FALSE` without inserting a map record; `Process_FcpInsert` owns the FCP record allocation while it holds `Process_ListLock`, and allocation failure releases `Process_ListLock`, restores IRQL, and returns; DFP/FCP delete, check, map key, silent flag, box-name copy, and force-policy semantics are unchanged. |
| Topology | `Session_Api_DisableForce` calls `Process_DfpInsert(PROCESS_TERMINATED, ProcessId)`, which raises IRQL, acquires `Process_ListLock`, deletes any old DFP record, allocates `FORCE_PROCESS_2`, inserts it into `Process_MapDfp`, then releases the lock and restores IRQL; allocation failure now exits through the same lock/IRQL owner. `Process_NotifyProcess_Create` calls `Process_DfpInsert(ParentId, ProcessId)` while already owning the process-list lock; allocation failure returns `FALSE` to that caller-owned lock scope. `Session_Api_ForceChildren` calls `Process_FcpInsert`, which mirrors the self-locked DFP lifetime for `FORCE_PROCESS_3` and `Process_MapFcp`. |
| Logic Risk | Force-process state is checked on process creation and session API paths, so low-memory failures must fail closed without crashing the driver or leaving `Process_ListLock`/IRQL ownership unbalanced. The old code treated map-record allocation as infallible and wrote through a possibly null pointer. The correct local repair is an allocation lifetime gate at the map-record owner boundary, not a change to force-process policy, force-children semantics, map keys, delete/check behavior, or alert routing. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-122-process-force-map-allocation-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `ExAllocatePoolWithTag`, `ExAcquireResourceExclusiveLite`, `ExReleaseResourceLite`, `KeRaiseIrql`, and `KeLowerIrql` references. `docs/plan/srev-122-process-force-map-allocation-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `PROCESS_FORCE_MAP_ALLOCATION_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | `Process_DfpInsert(PROCESS_TERMINATED, ProcessId)` now checks the `FORCE_PROCESS_2` allocation before writing fields or inserting into `Process_MapDfp`; on failure it releases `Process_ListLock`, lowers IRQL, and returns `FALSE`. `Process_DfpInsert(ParentId, ProcessId)` now checks the child `FORCE_PROCESS_2` allocation before writing fields or inserting into `Process_MapDfp`; on failure it returns `FALSE` to the already-locked caller. `Process_FcpInsert` now checks the `FORCE_PROCESS_3` allocation before writing fields or inserting into `Process_MapFcp`; on failure it releases `Process_ListLock`, lowers IRQL, and returns. No DFP/FCP map key, delete path, check path, silent flag behavior, box-name copy shape, force-alert policy, force-children policy, or process-list lock ownership changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-122.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, local nullable pool allocation shape, `Process_DfpInsert(PROCESS_TERMINATED, ...)` allocation gate before field writes with balanced lock/IRQL release, parent-child DFP allocation gate before field writes with caller-lock ownership preserved, `Process_FcpInsert` allocation gate before field writes with balanced lock/IRQL release, stale unguarded allocation/write patterns removed, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-122.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows driver build for `process_force.c`, pool-allocation failure injection for each DFP/FCP insertion path, lock/IRQL balance observation on self-locked failures, no-null-map-insert observation on caller-locked DFP failure, and positive DFP/FCP smoke proving unchanged insert, check, delete, silent flag, and box-name propagation behavior. |

### SREV-123: SCM Create Request Allocation Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official Win32 heap-allocation and SCM create/start shape; needs Windows service-runtime build/fault-injection proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/scm_create.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-122. `Scm_AddBoxedService` allocated `names2` with `Dll_AllocTemp` and immediately copied the existing `SandboxedServices` `REG_MULTI_SZ` into it. `Scm_CreateServiceW` allocated the fake `SC_HANDLE` service-name buffer with `Dll_Alloc` and immediately wrote the `tzuk` marker and service name into it after creating the service key and adding the service to `SandboxedServices`. `Scm_StartBoxedService2` allocated a `SERVICE_RUN_REQ` with `Dll_Alloc`, immediately wrote request fields into it, and did not free that request buffer after `SbieDll_CallServer` returned. The local `Dll_AllocFromPool` may return `NULL` before the sandbox name is initialized, and its allocation shape is compatible with the official Win32 heap-allocation failure shape. |
| Data | `Scm_AddBoxedService`, `Scm_CreateServiceW`, `Scm_StartBoxedService2`, `Dll_AllocTemp`, `Dll_Alloc`, `Dll_Free`, `Scm_GetBoxedServices`, `SandboxedServices`, `REG_MULTI_SZ`, fake `SC_HANDLE` service-name buffer, `SERVICE_RUN_REQ`, `SbieDll_CallServer`, `ERROR_NOT_ENOUGH_MEMORY`, `STATUS_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES`, `NtSetValueKey`, `NtDeleteKey`, `NtClose`, and special-service heap path cleanup. |
| Schema | `SCM_CREATE_REQUEST_ALLOCATION_LIFETIME` says `Dll_Alloc` and `Dll_AllocTemp` may fail before returning writable storage; `Scm_AddBoxedService` writes `names2` only after `Dll_AllocTemp` succeeds; `Scm_CreateServiceW` writes the fake service-handle name only after `Dll_Alloc` succeeds; `Scm_CreateServiceW` allocates the fake service handle before `Scm_AddBoxedService` mutates `SandboxedServices`; `Scm_CreateServiceW` frees the fake service handle if `Scm_AddBoxedService` fails; `Scm_StartBoxedService2` fills `SERVICE_RUN_REQ` only after `Dll_Alloc` succeeds; `Scm_StartBoxedService2` frees `SERVICE_RUN_REQ` after `SbieDll_CallServer` returns; service creation policy, registry values, sandboxed-service list semantics, special service routing, and service-run wire shape are unchanged. |
| Topology | `Scm_AddBoxedService` flows from `Scm_GetBoxedServices()` to a newly allocated `names2` buffer, appends the requested service name, writes the `SandboxedServices` `REG_MULTI_SZ`, and frees `names2`. `Scm_CreateServiceW` creates and populates the service registry key, allocates and fills the fake `SC_HANDLE` payload, then mutates the boxed-service list; on later list failure it frees the fake handle buffer before deleting the partial service key. `Scm_StartBoxedService2` derives the service path, allocates `SERVICE_RUN_REQ + path`, fills `MSGID_SERVICE_RUN`, sends it through `SbieDll_CallServer`, frees the request, and returns the server Win32 status. |
| Logic Risk | The old allocation-free shape mixed persistent service creation with unchecked transient buffers. A null `names2` or fake-handle buffer could crash during service creation. Adding the service to `SandboxedServices` before proving the fake handle buffer existed could also leave registry/list state mutated without a handle to return. The service-run path had both a null-write risk and a normal-path request leak. The correct local fix is allocation and ownership gating at these data nodes, not a change to SCM access policy, service registry value semantics, special boxed-service routing, or server wire validation. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-123-scm-create-request-allocation-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `HeapAlloc`, `HeapFree`, `CreateServiceW`, and `StartServiceW` references. `docs/plan/srev-123-scm-create-request-allocation-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `SCM_CREATE_REQUEST_ALLOCATION_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | `Scm_AddBoxedService` now checks `names2` after `Dll_AllocTemp` and returns `STATUS_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES` through the existing mutex/list cleanup path before writing the updated `REG_MULTI_SZ`. `Scm_CreateServiceW` now checks the fake service-handle name allocation before writing into it, performs that allocation before `Scm_AddBoxedService`, frees the handle-name buffer if the list update fails, and returns `ERROR_NOT_ENOUGH_MEMORY` on allocation failure. `Scm_StartBoxedService2` now checks the `SERVICE_RUN_REQ` allocation before filling it, frees any special-service heap path on allocation failure, and frees the request buffer after `SbieDll_CallServer` returns. No service creation policy, registry value shape, sandboxed-service list key, special service name routing, device map capture, request `msgid`, service run path copy, or call-server reply handling changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-123.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, local nullable `Dll_Alloc` / `Dll_AllocTemp` shape, `Scm_AddBoxedService` `names2` gate before writes, `Scm_CreateServiceW` fake-handle allocation before writes and before `Scm_AddBoxedService`, fake-handle cleanup on list-update failure, `Scm_StartBoxedService2` request allocation gate before writes, `SERVICE_RUN_REQ` cleanup after `SbieDll_CallServer`, stale unguarded allocation/write patterns removed, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-123.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows build for `scm_create.c`, allocation-failure injection for each patched allocation path, no stale `SandboxedServices` entry on fake-handle allocation failure, fake-handle cleanup on later list-update failure, request cleanup on service-run call-server return, and positive boxed-service create/start smoke proving unchanged registry values, list update, service-run request, and reply handling. |

### SREV-124: SbieApi Ioctl Close Request Boundary

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official native handle close, file open, and device-ioctl shape; needs Windows build/kmdutil runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/sbieapi.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-123. `SbieApi_Ioctl` uses a global cached `SbieApi_DeviceHandle` to open `API_DEVICE_NAME` with `NtOpenFile` and send synchronous driver API calls through `__sys_NtDeviceIoControlFile` or `NtDeviceIoControlFile`. The inline comment defines `parms == NULL` as a close request used by kmdutil. The old branch closed and invalidated the cached handle but then fell through into the trace and ioctl path, which reads `parms[0]`, can reopen the driver device, and can pass a null input buffer to the ioctl call. Microsoft documents `ZwClose` / `NtClose` as closing an object handle and returning an `NTSTATUS`, `NtOpenFile` as returning a file/device handle that must be closed when no longer in use, and `NtDeviceIoControlFile` as sending an ioctl to a specified file/device handle with an optional input buffer whose length must be zero when the buffer is null. |
| Data | `SbieApi_Ioctl`, `parms`, `parms[0]`, `SbieApi_DeviceHandle`, `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`, `NtClose`, `NtOpenFile`, `API_DEVICE_NAME`, `STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND`, `STATUS_NO_SUCH_DEVICE`, `STATUS_SERVER_DISABLED`, `Dll_SbieTrace`, `SbieApi_MonitorPutMsg`, `__sys_NtDeviceIoControlFile`, `NtDeviceIoControlFile`, `API_SBIEDRV_CTLCODE`, and `IO_STATUS_BLOCK`. |
| Schema | `SBIEAPI_IOCTL_CLOSE_REQUEST_BOUNDARY` says `SbieApi_Ioctl(NULL)` is a close request used by kmdutil; the close request closes the cached driver handle when one exists; the close request always invalidates `SbieApi_DeviceHandle`; the close request returns before trace, open, or device-ioctl logic can read `parms[0]` or reopen the driver device; the close request reports `NtClose` status when a handle existed, otherwise `STATUS_SUCCESS`; non-null requests preserve trace, driver-open, `STATUS_SERVER_DISABLED` remap, hook-bypass, and ioctl topology. |
| Topology | Close-request flow is now `SbieApi_Ioctl(NULL)` to optional `NtClose(SbieApi_DeviceHandle)`, cached-handle invalidation, and immediate return. Normal API flow remains non-null `parms`, optional trace using `parms[0]`, lazy `NtOpenFile(API_DEVICE_NAME)` when the cached handle is invalid, missing-driver status remap, and ioctl dispatch through `__sys_NtDeviceIoControlFile` or `NtDeviceIoControlFile` with `API_SBIEDRV_CTLCODE`. |
| Logic Risk | A close request and an ioctl request are opposite transitions at the cached driver-handle boundary. Falling through after close converted a lifecycle operation into a null-pointer read and could reopen the device immediately after closing it. The correct local repair is a terminal close branch, not a change to API argument marshalling, trace filtering, open access, missing-driver status mapping, hook bypass, or ioctl control code. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-124-sbieapi-ioctl-close-request-boundary.md` records Microsoft `ZwClose` / `NtClose`, `NtOpenFile`, `NtDeviceIoControlFile`, and Nt/Zw native service references. `docs/plan/srev-124-sbieapi-ioctl-close-request-boundary.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `SBIEAPI_IOCTL_CLOSE_REQUEST_BOUNDARY` contract. |
| Fix | `SbieApi_Ioctl(NULL)` now initializes `status` to `STATUS_SUCCESS`, calls `NtClose` only when a cached handle exists, stores the `NtClose` status when it runs, invalidates `SbieApi_DeviceHandle`, and returns immediately. No non-null request trace logic, lazy driver open, missing-driver remap, hook-bypass selection, ioctl code, input-buffer shape, or return propagation changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-124.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, terminal close-request branch before trace/parms access, stale fallthrough close pattern removal, preservation of normal trace/open/remap/ioctl topology, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-124.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows build for `sbieapi.c`, kmdutil close-request smoke proving existing handles close without trace/open/ioctl fallthrough, null close request with no cached handle proving `STATUS_SUCCESS` and no reopen, and positive normal API call smoke proving unchanged trace filtering, lazy driver open, missing-driver remap, hook bypass, and ioctl request buffer shape. |

### SREV-125: File Copy Junction Handle State

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official native file-create, file-query, file-system-control, reparse-point, and handle-close shape; needs Windows junction migration runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/file_copy.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-124. `File_MigrateJunction` opens a source reparse point with `NtCreateFile`, queries `FILE_NETWORK_OPEN_INFORMATION`, reads the source reparse data with `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT`, creates a destination directory reparse point, sets the reparse data with `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT`, and then copies timestamps/attributes from `open_info`. The old source did not check the metadata query before later consuming `open_info`, returned directly after `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT` failure without closing `TrueHandle`, and after destination `NtCreateFile` failure closed/free'd local resources but fell through into `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT` with an invalid `CopyHandle`. Microsoft documents `NtCreateFile` as returning a file/directory handle that must later be closed, `NtQueryInformationFile` as returning `FILE_NETWORK_OPEN_INFORMATION` only through the caller buffer on success, `NtFsControlFile` as sending an FSCTL to a specified file handle, `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT` as requiring a non-null file handle and input buffer, and `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT` as returning status plus reparse data on successful completion. |
| Data | `File_MigrateJunction`, `TruePath`, `CopyPath`, `TrueHandle`, `CopyHandle`, `open_info`, `FILE_NETWORK_OPEN_INFORMATION`, `REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER`, `MAXIMUM_REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER_SIZE`, `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT`, `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT`, `NtCreateFile`, `NtQueryInformationFile`, `NtFsControlFile`, `NtClose`, `pSecurityDescriptor`, `File_AddCurrentUserToSD`, and `File_SetAttributes`. |
| Schema | `FILE_COPY_JUNCTION_HANDLE_STATE` says `File_MigrateJunction` opens the source reparse point before querying metadata or reparse data; `FILE_NETWORK_OPEN_INFORMATION` is used only after `NtQueryInformationFile(FileNetworkOpenInformation)` succeeds; source reparse data is used only after `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT` succeeds; every local failure after `TrueHandle` opens closes `TrueHandle` before returning; destination reparse data is set only after destination `NtCreateFile` succeeds and initializes `CopyHandle`; destination create failure frees copied security descriptor storage when one exists and returns before `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT`; reparse data shape, ACL copy policy, destination create options, and attribute-copy behavior are unchanged. |
| Topology | Successful junction migration flows from source `NtCreateFile(TruePath, FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT)`, to `NtQueryInformationFile(TrueHandle, FileNetworkOpenInformation)`, to `NtFsControlFile(TrueHandle, FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT)`, optional security descriptor copy, destination `NtCreateFile(CopyPath, FILE_CREATE | FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT)`, `NtFsControlFile(CopyHandle, FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT)`, `File_SetAttributes(CopyHandle, open_info)`, and final handle/security cleanup. Failure after source query, source get-reparse, or destination create now terminates at the owning cleanup edge instead of crossing into a later state. |
| Logic Risk | Native file and FSCTL calls publish handles or state only on success. Treating failed metadata query as if it had filled `open_info` can propagate uninitialized timestamps and attributes. Returning from a failed get-reparse call without closing `TrueHandle` leaks the source handle. Falling through after destination create failure is the largest edge error: it lets the set-reparse operation run against a destination handle that was never legally produced. The correct local repair is state gating and cleanup, not a change to junction migration policy or reparse-buffer semantics. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-125-file-copy-junction-handle-state.md` records Microsoft `NtCreateFile`, `NtQueryInformationFile`, `NtFsControlFile`, `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT`, `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT`, and `ZwClose` / `NtClose` references. `docs/plan/srev-125-file-copy-junction-handle-state.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `FILE_COPY_JUNCTION_HANDLE_STATE` contract. |
| Fix | `File_MigrateJunction` now checks `NtQueryInformationFile` before any reparse-data work and closes `TrueHandle` on query failure. It also closes `TrueHandle` before returning on `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT` failure. Destination `NtCreateFile` failure now closes `TrueHandle`, frees `pSecurityDescriptor` when allocated, and returns immediately before any `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT` call. No source open access, `FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` usage, reparse buffer sizing, destination create flags, ACL copy policy, attribute copy, or successful cleanup path changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-125.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, metadata query gate before reparse get, source get-reparse failure cleanup, destination create failure return before set-reparse, stale handle-leak/fallthrough patterns removed, preserved source/destination reparse topology, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-125.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows build for `file_copy.c`, junction migration positive smoke, source `NtQueryInformationFile` failure injection proving `open_info` is not consumed and `TrueHandle` closes, source `FSCTL_GET_REPARSE_POINT` failure injection proving `TrueHandle` closes, destination `NtCreateFile` failure injection proving no `FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT` with invalid `CopyHandle`, and security descriptor cleanup observation when allocated. |

### SREV-126: GUI Enum Window Station Handle Ownership

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official window-station get/create/close and handle-duplication shape; needs Windows GUI runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/dll/guienum.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-125. `Gui_CreateWindowStationW` and `Gui_CreateWindowStationA` first call the native `CreateWindowStationW/A`; if that fails, selected browser / `UseSbieWndStation` fallback paths returned `Gui_Dummy_WinSta`. That dummy handle is captured in `Sandboxie/core/dll/gui.c` from `GetProcessWindowStation()` and then set as the process window station. Microsoft documents `GetProcessWindowStation` as returning the current process window-station handle and explicitly says not to close that handle. Microsoft documents `CreateWindowStationW` as returning a handle that must be freed with `CloseWindowStation`, and `CloseWindowStation` says its input should be a handle returned by `CreateWindowStation` or `OpenWindowStation`, not `GetProcessWindowStation`. Microsoft documents `DuplicateHandle` as producing a duplicate handle value valid in the target process, including window-station handles returned by `GetProcessWindowStation`, with a nonzero return on success. |
| Data | `Gui_CreateWindowStationW`, `Gui_CreateWindowStationA`, `Gui_Dummy_WinSta`, `GetProcessWindowStation`, `CreateWindowStationW`, `CreateWindowStationA`, `CloseWindowStation`, `DuplicateHandle`, `GetCurrentProcess`, `SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES.bInheritHandle`, `DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS`, `UseSbieWndStation`, `DLL_IMAGE_GOOGLE_CHROME`, `DLL_IMAGE_MOZILLA_FIREFOX`, and `SbieApi_Log(2205, L"CreateWindowStation")`. |
| Schema | `GUIENUM_WINDOWSTATION_HANDLE_OWNERSHIP` says `Gui_Dummy_WinSta` is a process window-station handle captured from the process window-station topology; `GetProcessWindowStation` returned handles are process-owned and must not be closed by ordinary callers; `CreateWindowStationW/A` returned handles are caller-owned and releasable with `CloseWindowStation`; the fallback must not return `Gui_Dummy_WinSta` directly; when fallback uses `Gui_Dummy_WinSta`, it returns a duplicate handle produced by `DuplicateHandle` in the current process with `DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS`; duplicate inheritability follows `SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES.bInheritHandle` when `lpsa` is supplied; native `CreateWindowStationW/A`, fallback policy predicates, logging, and failure return shape are unchanged. |
| Topology | The native path remains `Gui_CreateWindowStationW/A` to `__sys_CreateWindowStationW/A(...)` and direct native handle return on success. The fallback path is now `Gui_Dummy_WinSta` policy gate, derive inheritability from `lpsa`, duplicate `Gui_Dummy_WinSta` into the current process with `DuplicateHandle`, and return the duplicate as the caller-owned fallback handle. Duplicate failure flows to the existing log-and-zero failure path. |
| Logic Risk | The old fallback crossed incompatible handle ownership schemas. A `GetProcessWindowStation` handle is process-associated state and must not be closed, but a `CreateWindowStation` return value is caller-owned and expected to be closed with `CloseWindowStation`. Returning the dummy handle directly let a normal caller follow the public API contract and close or attempt to close Sandboxie's process window-station state. The correct repair is a duplicate at the fallback boundary, not a change to the policy deciding when fallback is used. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-126-guienum-windowstation-handle-ownership.md` records Microsoft `GetProcessWindowStation`, `CreateWindowStationW`, `CloseWindowStation`, and `DuplicateHandle` references. `docs/plan/srev-126-guienum-windowstation-handle-ownership.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `GUIENUM_WINDOWSTATION_HANDLE_OWNERSHIP` contract. |
| Fix | `Gui_CreateWindowStationW` and `Gui_CreateWindowStationA` now duplicate `Gui_Dummy_WinSta` with `DuplicateHandle(GetCurrentProcess(), Gui_Dummy_WinSta, GetCurrentProcess(), &hDuplicate, 0, inherit, DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS)` before returning it from fallback. `inherit` is derived from `lpsa->bInheritHandle` when `lpsa` exists. If duplication fails, the functions keep the existing `SbieApi_Log(2205, L"CreateWindowStation")` and `return 0` failure path. No native `CreateWindowStationW/A` call, fallback predicate, browser exception, log message, or non-fallback behavior changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-126.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, `Gui_Dummy_WinSta` source topology, duplicate-handle fallback in both W/A paths, inheritability preservation from `SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES`, stale direct `return Gui_Dummy_WinSta` removal, native direct-return preservation, fallback policy preservation, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-126.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows GUI build for `guienum.c`, fallback smoke proving returned handle is a duplicate and `CloseWindowStation` on it does not close `Gui_Dummy_WinSta`, inheritability matrix for null/false/true `lpsa`, native success smoke proving unchanged direct return, and duplicate failure injection proving log-and-zero failure behavior. |

### SREV-127: Named Pipe LPC Name Wire String

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official CRT wide-string comparison/copy shape; needs Windows service LPC/ALPC runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/namedpipeserver.cpp` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-126. `OpenHandler` already terminates `NAMED_PIPE_OPEN_REQ::name` and `server` after validating the full fixed request. `LpcConnectHandler` accepted `NAMED_PIPE_LPC_CONNECT_REQ`, then used `req->name` in `_wcsicmp` allow-list checks and `wcscat(port_name, req->name)` without first forcing the fixed wire array to be null-terminated. Microsoft documents `_wcsicmp` inputs as null-terminated strings and `wcscpy` inputs as null-terminated source strings copied including the terminator. |
| Data | `LpcConnectHandler`, `NAMED_PIPE_LPC_CONNECT_REQ`, `req->name[64]`, `_wcsicmp`, `wcscpy`, `wcscat`, `port_name[96]`, `\\RPC Control\\`, `ntsvcs`, `plugplay`, `NtConnectPort`, `NtAlpcConnectPort`, `info_len`, `info_data`, `OpenHandler`, `NAMED_PIPE_OPEN_REQ`, `req->server`, and `ARRAYSIZE`. |
| Schema | `NAMEDPIPE_LPC_NAME_WIRE_STRING` says `NAMED_PIPE_LPC_CONNECT_REQ::name` is a fixed-size wire field, not a trusted C wide string until the server terminates it; `LpcConnectHandler` validates the full fixed request header before writing the local terminator into `req->name`; `LpcConnectHandler` writes NUL to the last element of `req->name` before `_wcsicmp`, `wcscpy`, or `wcscat` consumes it; the allow-list remains limited to `ntsvcs` and `plugplay`; `\\RPC Control\\` object path composition, old LPC vs ALPC branch, info buffer validation, and proxy-handle ownership are unchanged; `OpenHandler` remains the nearby precedent for terminating fixed wire string fields before string operations. |
| Topology | A sandboxed caller sends `NAMED_PIPE_LPC_CONNECT_REQ` through PipeServer. The service validates `req->h.length >= sizeof(NAMED_PIPE_LPC_CONNECT_REQ)`, terminates `req->name[ARRAYSIZE(req->name)-1]`, applies the `_wcsicmp` allow-list, composes `\\RPC Control\\<name>` into `port_name`, validates `info_len` / `info_data`, then connects through `NtConnectPort` for old LPC or `NtAlpcConnectPort` for ALPC and stores the resulting port in `m_ProxyHandle`. |
| Logic Risk | Fixed wire arrays and CRT wide-string operations have different schemas. A caller can fill all 64 WCHAR slots in `req->name` without a terminator while still satisfying the fixed request length. The old code then let `_wcsicmp` and `wcscat` read past the wire field while deciding whether to broker access to privileged RPC Control LPC endpoints. The correct repair is the same local terminator gate already present in `OpenHandler`, not an allow-list expansion, port-name redesign, or LPC/ALPC behavior change. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-127-namedpipe-lpc-name-wire-string.md` records Microsoft `_wcsicmp` and `wcscpy` / wide-string copy references. `docs/plan/srev-127-namedpipe-lpc-name-wire-string.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `NAMEDPIPE_LPC_NAME_WIRE_STRING` contract. |
| Fix | `LpcConnectHandler` now writes `req->name[ARRAYSIZE(req->name) - 1] = L'\0';` immediately after the fixed request-size gate succeeds and before any `_wcsicmp` allow-list checks. No allow-list entries, `\\RPC Control\\` prefix, `wcscpy` / `wcscat` composition, old LPC path, ALPC path, info buffer validation, returned handle ownership, or cleanup path changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-127.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, wire schema, `OpenHandler` precedent, `LpcConnectHandler` terminator placement before `_wcsicmp`, preservation of allow-list/path/LPC/ALPC/proxy-handle topology, stale unterminated old shape removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-127.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows service build for `namedpipeserver.cpp`, LPC connect positive smoke for `ntsvcs` and `plugplay`, negative request with all 64 `name` WCHAR slots nonzero proving bounded read and deny behavior, ALPC branch smoke where supported, and regression smoke proving named-pipe `OpenHandler` behavior is unchanged. |

### SREV-128: GUI XP Hook Trampoline Failure Lifetime

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official pool allocation/free lifetime shape and local trampoline NULL-return shape; needs Windows XP hook runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/gui_xp.c` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-127. `Gui_HookService` builds trampolines for XP GUI and IPC service hooks, optionally patches a SpySweeper push-jump trampoline, publishes `*pSourceFunc = Trampoline`, builds a hook entry, and then modifies kernel code under MDL/DPC freeze control. `Hook_BuildTramp` in `Sandboxie/core/dll/hook_tramp.c` can return `NULL` when instruction analysis fails, trampoline allocation fails, or trampoline copy fails. The old code wrote through `Trampoline` in the `push_jmp_target` branch before checking `if (! Trampoline)`. The same failure path freed `context` with `ExFreePoolWithTag(context, tzuk)` and then restored `*pSourceFunc = context->SaveBytesAddr`. Microsoft documents `ExAllocatePoolWithTag` as returning `NULL` on insufficient pool memory and `ExFreePool` as releasing pool memory that must not be accessed after it is freed; the same page points `ExAllocatePoolWithTag` buffers to `ExFreePoolWithTag`. |
| Data | `Gui_HookService`, `pSourceFunc`, `OriginalSourceFunc`, `pJumpStub`, `TargetFunc`, `GUI_HOOKSERVICE_CONTEXT`, `SaveBytesAddr`, `Trampoline`, `Hook_BuildTramp`, `push_jmp_target`, `Hook_BuildJump`, `Process_BuildHookEntry`, `Gui_HookFreeze`, `ExFreePoolWithTag`, and `STATUS_UNSUCCESSFUL`. |
| Schema | `GUI_XP_HOOK_TRAMPOLINE_FAILURE_LIFETIME` says `Gui_HookService` stores the original source function pointer in owner-local storage before any failure edge can free the hook context; `GUI_HOOKSERVICE_CONTEXT::SaveBytesAddr` mirrors the original source function pointer but is not the failure-restore owner after context cleanup; `Hook_BuildTramp` may return `NULL` when instruction analysis or trampoline allocation/copy fails; `Gui_HookService` checks the `Hook_BuildTramp` result before SpySweeper push-jump trampoline writes through it; `push_jmp_target` special trampoline patching only runs after `Trampoline` is non-null; failure cleanup frees `GUI_HOOKSERVICE_CONTEXT` only before restoring `pSourceFunc` from `OriginalSourceFunc`; failure cleanup never dereferences `context` after `ExFreePoolWithTag(context, tzuk)`; successful hook topology and `Process_BuildHookEntry` behavior are unchanged; XP win32k service discovery, hotfix prolog handling, MDL locking, and DPC freeze topology are unchanged. |
| Topology | The successful path remains original service pointer snapshot, prolog analysis, `Hook_BuildTramp`, optional push-jump patch, `*pSourceFunc = Trampoline`, `Process_BuildHookEntry`, MDL writable mapping, DPC freeze, and `Hook_BuildJump`. The corrected failure path snapshots `OriginalSourceFunc`, lets any failure flow through freeze/thread cleanup and `ExFreePoolWithTag(context)`, then restores `*pJumpStub = 0` and `*pSourceFunc = OriginalSourceFunc` without reading freed context storage. |
| Logic Risk | Kernel hook installation is a state machine over executable pointers. A trampoline pointer is not legal until `Hook_BuildTramp` returns non-null; writing push/jump bytes before that gate can become a kernel NULL write on allocation or decode failure. `context->SaveBytesAddr` contains the right value, but after `ExFreePoolWithTag(context, tzuk)` the context no longer owns readable storage. The correct local repair is to preserve the original source pointer in a stack variable and restore from it after cleanup, not to change hook policy or trampoline construction. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-128-gui-xp-hook-trampoline-failure-lifetime.md` records Microsoft `ExAllocatePoolWithTag` and `ExFreePool` references plus the local `Hook_BuildTramp` NULL-return shape. `docs/plan/srev-128-gui-xp-hook-trampoline-failure-lifetime.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `GUI_XP_HOOK_TRAMPOLINE_FAILURE_LIFETIME` contract. |
| Fix | `Gui_HookService` now captures `OriginalSourceFunc = *pSourceFunc` before allocating the context, initializes `context->SaveBytesAddr` from that snapshot, checks `Hook_BuildTramp` immediately before the `push_jmp_target` write block, and restores `*pSourceFunc = OriginalSourceFunc` on failure after `context` has been freed. No service discovery, hotfix prolog detection, trampoline construction policy, hook entry generation, MDL locking, DPC freeze, successful jump write, or retry topology changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-128.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, local `Hook_BuildTramp` NULL-return shape, original source pointer snapshot, immediate trampoline null gate before `push_jmp_target`, stale post-free `context->SaveBytesAddr` restore removal, unchanged successful hook topology, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-128.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows build for `gui_xp.c`, XP hook install smoke for all affected services, failure injection where `Hook_BuildTramp` returns `NULL` on a `push_jmp_target` prolog proving no NULL write and clean `FALSE` return, failure injection after `*pSourceFunc = Trampoline` proving restore to original pointer, and Driver Verifier or kernel debugger observation proving no freed context access. |

### SREV-129: NetApi UseAdd Auth Identity Length Gate

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official NetUseAdd / USE_INFO data-shape review; needs Windows NetUseAdd broker runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/netapiserver.cpp` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-128. `Sandboxie/core/svc/netapiwire.h` defines `NETAPI_USE_ADD_REQ` as a fixed wire packet with `ui4_auth_identity[2048+2]`, and `Sandboxie/core/dll/netapi.c` constructs that request from client `USE_INFO_*` input. `NetApiServer::UseAdd` receives the packet, converts local fields into a `USE_INFO_*` view, impersonates the caller, calls `NetUseAdd(NULL, req->level, (UCHAR *)&info, &parm_index)`, then starts `LaunchSlave` only on success. The string length validators set `parm_index` and stop before impersonation, but the old `ui4_auth_identity_length > 2048` branch only set `error_code = ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` and continued. The later impersonation and `NetUseAdd` call overwrote `error_code` and consumed a partially initialized local `USE_INFO_4`. Microsoft documents `NetUseAdd` as receiving a `buf` whose format depends on Level, and documents `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` / `parm_err` as the invalid `USE_INFO_*` member signal. Microsoft documents `USE_INFO_2` string pointer members and `USE_INFO_3` as nesting `USE_INFO_2` plus `ui3_flags`; the local level-4 extension follows the same validated-wire-to-pointer-view topology. |
| Data | `NetApiServer::UseAdd`, `NETAPI_USE_ADD_REQ`, `NETAPI_USE_ADD_RPL`, `req->level`, `ui4_auth_identity_length`, `ui4_auth_identity[2048+2]`, local `USE_INFO_4`, `error_code`, `parm_index`, `PipeServer::ImpersonateCaller`, `NetUseAdd`, `LaunchSlave`, and client-side `NetApi_NetUseAdd`. |
