| Schema | `NETAPI_USEADD_AUTH_IDENTITY_LENGTH_GATE` says `UseAdd` validates every `NETAPI_USE_ADD_REQ` wire field before `PipeServer::ImpersonateCaller` or `NetUseAdd` can run; `ui4_auth_identity_length` uses the fixed 2048 byte wire buffer limit; `ULONG -1` remains the null auth identity sentinel; values greater than 2048 set `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` and exit before impersonation; valid auth identity bytes are locally terminated before `USE_INFO_4` points at the wire buffer; `parm_index` based string length failures still return `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` before impersonation; `NetUseAdd` receives only a `USE_INFO` shaped buffer assembled from validated local wire fields; `LaunchSlave` still runs only after `NetUseAdd` succeeds; NetUseAdd level policy, local string limits, password handling, and drive notification topology are unchanged. |
| Topology | Legal flow is fixed `NETAPI_USE_ADD_REQ`, validate `h.length` and `level`, validate/cap local, remote, password, username, domain, and auth identity fields, assemble a local `USE_INFO_*` pointer view into the fixed wire buffers, impersonate caller, call `NetUseAdd`, return `NETAPI_USE_ADD_RPL`, and optionally run `LaunchSlave` only when `NetUseAdd` succeeds. Invalid `ui4_auth_identity_length > 2048` now flows directly to the reply path before impersonation and before `NetUseAdd`. |
| Logic Risk | The old code confused setting an error value with owning the transition. Since `error_code` was overwritten later, an impossible wire length could still cross into the privileged service broker and native NetAPI call. Because the valid branch also initializes the local `USE_INFO_4` pointer and length fields, skipping that branch left the level-4 view outside a legal state. The correct local repair is a validation barrier before impersonation, not a policy change to NetUseAdd levels or drive notification. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-129-netapi-useadd-auth-identity-length-gate.md` records Microsoft `NetUseAdd`, `USE_INFO_2`, and `USE_INFO_3` references. `docs/plan/srev-129-netapi-useadd-auth-identity-length-gate.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `NETAPI_USEADD_AUTH_IDENTITY_LENGTH_GATE` contract. |
| Fix | `NetApiServer::UseAdd` now checks `if (error_code) goto finish;` after level-4 field validation and before the existing `parm_index` gate. Invalid `ui4_auth_identity_length > 2048` therefore returns `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` without impersonating the caller or calling `NetUseAdd`. Valid auth identity data, `ULONG -1` null sentinel behavior, existing string length parameter errors, `NetUseAdd` call shape, reply shape, and success-only `LaunchSlave` behavior are unchanged. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-129.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, wire shape, client request construction precedent, invalid auth identity length gate before impersonation, existing `parm_index` gate preservation, valid auth identity termination and pointer assignment, `NetUseAdd` call topology, success-only `LaunchSlave`, stale ungated pattern removal, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-129.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows service build for `netapiserver.cpp`, valid `NetUseAdd` mapping smoke, malformed level-4 request with `ui4_auth_identity_length = 2049` proving no `NetUseAdd` call and `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`, null sentinel request proving unchanged behavior, and 2048-byte valid auth identity request proving unchanged forwarding. |

### SREV-130: DriverAssist InjectLow Process Handle State

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official OpenProcess / CloseHandle / GetProcessTimes handle-state review; needs Windows injection runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/DriverAssistInject.cpp` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-129. `DriverAssist::InjectLow` can exit before opening the target process when `m_DriverReady` is false, but its shared `finish` block checks `if (hProcess)` before reporting an injection failure to the driver and closing the process handle. The old code declared `HANDLE hProcess = InjectLow_OpenProcess(_msg);` after that early exit, so the cleanup join point depended on a process-handle variable that did not have an initialized owner state on every edge into `finish`. Microsoft documents `OpenProcess` as returning an open process handle on success and `NULL` on failure, and says the returned handle should be closed with `CloseHandle` when finished. Microsoft documents `CloseHandle` as taking a valid handle to an open object. Microsoft documents `GetProcessTimes` as requiring a process handle with query rights and returning process timing data only on success. `InjectLow_OpenProcess` follows that shape by opening a process, validating creation time, returning the handle only on match, otherwise closing the temporary handle and returning `NULL`. |
| Data | `DriverAssist::InjectLow`, `DriverAssist::InjectLow_OpenProcess`, `hProcess`, `m_DriverReady`, `errlvl`, `SVC_PROCESS_MSG::process_id`, `SVC_PROCESS_MSG::create_time`, `OpenProcess`, `GetProcessTimes`, `CloseHandle`, `SbieDll_InjectLow`, `GuiServer::InitProcess`, `MountManager::AcquireBoxRoot`, `SbieApi_Call(API_INJECT_COMPLETE, ...)`, `file_root_path`, and `reg_root_path`. |
| Schema | `DRIVERASSIST_INJECT_PROCESS_HANDLE_STATE` says `InjectLow` owns a process handle state variable initialized to `NULL` before any `finish` edge; `InjectLow_OpenProcess` returns a real process handle only after `OpenProcess` succeeds and creation time matches; `OpenProcess` failure and creation-time mismatch leave `InjectLow::hProcess` as `NULL`; `finish` cleanup tests only initialized `hProcess` before `API_INJECT_COMPLETE` failure notification and `CloseHandle`; `CloseHandle` is called only for a non-null process handle produced by `InjectLow_OpenProcess`; driver-not-ready early exit does not read an uninitialized process handle; path allocation cleanup remains independent from process handle cleanup; successful injection, `GuiServer`, `MountManager`, and `API_INJECT_COMPLETE` topology are unchanged; failed injection still reports `API_INJECT_COMPLETE` error only when a target process handle was legally opened. |
| Topology | Legal flow is `hProcess = NULL`, optional `InjectLow_OpenProcess`, `OpenProcess` success plus `GetProcessTimes` creation-time match, `hProcess` owns the target process handle, injection / GUI / mount / driver-complete path, then `finish` closes the handle once. Driver-not-ready and other pre-open failures flow to `finish` with `hProcess == NULL`, so no process-handle notification and no `CloseHandle` run. |
| Logic Risk | `finish` is a resource cleanup join point for paths before and after process-handle acquisition. A join point needs a legal state value for every resource it tests. The old source made `hProcess` legal only on the acquisition path, but `finish` also runs from pre-acquisition paths. In a service-side process injection broker, reading an uninitialized handle value can turn an early failure into a false `API_INJECT_COMPLETE` failure notification or an invalid `CloseHandle`. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-130-driverassist-inject-process-handle-state.md` records Microsoft `OpenProcess`, `CloseHandle`, and `GetProcessTimes` references. `docs/plan/srev-130-driverassist-inject-process-handle-state.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `DRIVERASSIST_INJECT_PROCESS_HANDLE_STATE` contract. |
| Fix | `DriverAssist::InjectLow` now declares `HANDLE hProcess = NULL;` with the other resource state variables before the first `goto finish`. The later open step assigns `hProcess = InjectLow_OpenProcess(_msg);`. No injection policy, process access mask, creation-time verification, path cleanup, GUI job assignment, mount, driver notification success path, or failure logging format changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-130.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, handle owner initialization before the first finish edge, assignment from `InjectLow_OpenProcess`, absence of the stale declaration-at-open pattern, `InjectLow_OpenProcess` open/time-match/close topology, successful injection topology preservation, failure cleanup preservation, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-130.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows service build for `DriverAssistInject.cpp`, driver-not-ready injection request proving no invalid handle read, `OpenProcess` failure injection proving cleanup skips process-handle actions, creation-time mismatch proving the temporary handle closes inside `InjectLow_OpenProcess`, and later failure after successful open proving exactly one failure notification and one close. |

### SREV-131: Process FindSandboxed Lock Release

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official ERESOURCE / IRQL release-shape review; needs Windows XP_SUPPORT driver runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/drv/process.h` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-130. `Sandboxie/core/drv/process.c` implements `Process_Find` by raising IRQL to `APC_LEVEL`, acquiring `Process_ListLock`, and either releasing/lowering locally when `out_irql` is null or transferring the old IRQL and held lock to the caller when `out_irql` is non-null. `Process_FindSandboxed` wraps that API and filters host-injection processes by returning `NULL` for `proc->bHostInject`. Before this SREV, the filtered `NULL` path did not release `Process_ListLock` or lower IRQL when `Process_Find` had transferred ownership through `out_irql`. Microsoft documents `ExAcquireResourceSharedLite` as acquiring a resource and says it can be released with `ExReleaseResourceLite`; Microsoft documents `ExReleaseResourceLite` as releasing an executive resource owned by the current thread; Microsoft documents `KeRaiseIrql` as storing the original IRQL for later `KeLowerIrql`; Microsoft documents `KeLowerIrql` as restoring the IRQL value returned by the preceding raise call. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/drv/process.h`, `Sandboxie/core/drv/process.c`, `Process_Find`, `Process_FindSandboxed`, `Process_ListLock`, `Process_Map`, `PROCESS`, `PROCESS_TERMINATED`, `out_irql`, `KIRQL irql`, `KeRaiseIrql`, `KeLowerIrql`, `ExAcquireResourceSharedLite`, `ExReleaseResourceLite`, and `proc->bHostInject`. |
| Schema | `PROCESS_FINDSANDBOXED_LOCK_RELEASE` says `Process_Find` owns the `Process_ListLock` and raised-IRQL transfer contract when `out_irql` is non-null; `Process_Find` raises to `APC_LEVEL` and acquires `Process_ListLock` before returning a protected `PROCESS *`; `Process_Find` without `out_irql` releases `Process_ListLock` and lowers IRQL before return; `Process_Find` with `out_irql` stores the old IRQL for the caller and leaves `Process_ListLock` held; `Process_FindSandboxed` may filter a found `PROCESS` when `bHostInject` is set; filtered `NULL` after `bHostInject` releases `Process_ListLock` and lowers IRQL when `out_irql` is non-null; direct `NULL` misses and `PROCESS_TERMINATED` sentinel behavior are preserved; successful sandboxed `PROCESS` ownership and caller-release topology are unchanged. |
| Topology | Legal flow is `Process_Find(..., &irql)`, raise to `APC_LEVEL`, acquire `Process_ListLock`, find `PROCESS`, write `*out_irql`, and transfer caller-owned release/lower while the caller inspects the protected process. The corrected host-inject filter path is `Process_FindSandboxed(..., &irql)`, `Process_Find` transfers lock/IRQL, `proc` exists and is not `PROCESS_TERMINATED`, `proc->bHostInject` is true, release `Process_ListLock`, lower IRQL using `*out_irql`, then return `NULL`. Direct misses and `PROCESS_TERMINATED` returns keep their existing semantics. |
| Logic Risk | `Process_FindSandboxed` changes "found process" into "no sandboxed process" for host-injection records. Returning `NULL` while still transferring lock/IRQL state leaves the caller with no process object but a hidden kernel cleanup obligation, which can leak `Process_ListLock` ownership or keep IRQL raised on future use of the wrapper contract. The correct owner-local repair is to balance the lower-level transfer only on the filtered path, not to alter sandbox membership policy or the `PROCESS_TERMINATED` sentinel. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-131-process-findsandboxed-lock-release.md` records Microsoft `ExAcquireResourceSharedLite`, `ExReleaseResourceLite`, `KeRaiseIrql`, and `KeLowerIrql` references. `docs/plan/srev-131-process-findsandboxed-lock-release.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `PROCESS_FINDSANDBOXED_LOCK_RELEASE` contract. |
| Fix | `Process_FindSandboxed` now releases `Process_ListLock` and lowers IRQL through `*out_irql` before returning `NULL` for a `bHostInject` filtered process when the caller requested the locked return contract. Normal sandboxed process returns, direct misses, and `PROCESS_TERMINATED` returns are unchanged. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-131.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, `Process_Find` lock/IRQL transfer shape, `Process_FindSandboxed` host-inject filter shape, release/lower ordering before `proc = NULL`, preservation of direct miss and `PROCESS_TERMINATED` topology, stale filtered-NULL-without-release shape removal, header declaration, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-131.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows driver build with `XP_SUPPORT` enabled for `process.c`, normal sandboxed lookup proving caller-owned release remains intact, `bHostInject` filtered lookup proving `NULL` return does not leak `Process_ListLock` or raised IRQL, `PROCESS_TERMINATED` lookup proving sentinel behavior is unchanged, and Driver Verifier or kernel debugger observation proving no leaked ERESOURCE ownership. |

### SREV-132: Low ARM64 Entry Syscall ABI Contract

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [minor] |
| Status | reviewed source-level ABI/topology classification after official Windows ARM64 / ARM64EC ABI review; needs Windows ARM64 and ARM64EC runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/low/entry_arm.asm` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-131. It exports `SystemServiceARM64`, `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, `DeviceIoControlSvc`, `EcExitThunkPtr`, `DetourCodeARM64`, and `SbieLowData`. `SystemServiceARM64` spills incoming `x0-x7` syscall arguments, records the syscall index from `x17`, builds an `API_INVOKE_SYSCALL` parameter block, and calls the local `SBIELOW_DATA.NtDeviceIoControlFile` pointer. `Sandboxie/core/low/init.c` patches the `ServiceDataPtr` slot immediately before `SystemServiceARM64`, and in ARM64EC mode routes `data->NtDeviceIoControlFile` to `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, copies the native `svc` instruction into `DeviceIoControlSvc`, and writes `EcExitThunkPtr` from the syscall extra-data trailer. Microsoft documents the Windows ARM64 volatile/non-volatile register split and 16-byte stack-alignment requirement. Microsoft documents Arm64EC as interoperating with x64 code in the same process, and describes entry/exit thunks and call-checker mediated ABI transitions. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/low/entry_arm.asm`, `Sandboxie/core/low/init.c`, `Sandboxie/core/low/lowdata.h`, `SystemServiceARM64`, `ServiceDataPtr`, `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, `DeviceIoControlSvc`, `EcExitThunkPtr`, `MyHandleStubHijack`, `DetourCodeARM64`, `SbieLowData`, `SBIELOW_DATA`, `api_device_handle`, `api_sbiedrv_ctlcode`, `api_invoke_syscall`, `NtDeviceIoControlFile`, `NtDeviceIoControlFile_code`, `RealNtDeviceIoControlFile`, `syscall_data`, `x0-x17`, `sp`, `fp`, `lr`, and `API_NUM_ARGS`. |
| Schema | `LOW_ARM64_ENTRY_SYSCALL_ABI_CONTRACT` says `SystemServiceARM64` is an ARM64 syscall bridge that follows the Windows ARM64 volatile register and 16-byte stack-alignment contract; it spills original `x0-x7` syscall arguments before repurposing `x0-x7` for `NtDeviceIoControlFile`; it records the syscall index from `x17` and the original argument-stack pointer in `API_NUM_ARGS` slots; it builds the `NtDeviceIoControlFile` call with `x0-x7` plus two stack arguments and returns the call status in `x0`; `PrepSyscalls` patches the `ServiceDataPtr` slot immediately before `SystemServiceARM64`; ARM64EC routing points `data->NtDeviceIoControlFile` to `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, copies the native `svc` instruction into `DeviceIoControlSvc`, and writes `EcExitThunkPtr`; `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC` branches through `MyHandleStubHijack` only when the emulator changes the sentinel; this SREV does not change syscall detour bytes or ARM64EC thunk policy. |
| Topology | ARM64 syscall flow is patched `Zw*` export, `SystemServiceARM64`, spill original syscall args, `x17` syscall index plus original argument-stack pointer, `API_INVOKE_SYSCALL` parameter block, `SBIELOW_DATA.NtDeviceIoControlFile`, `SbieDrv` `API_SBIEDRV_CTLCODE`, and NTSTATUS return in `x0`. ARM64EC flow is `PrepSyscalls` seeing `is_arm64ec`, `data->NtDeviceIoControlFile = NtDeviceIoControlFileEC`, `DeviceIoControlSvc` receiving the native `svc` instruction, `EcExitThunkPtr` receiving the extra-data trailer thunk pointer, `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC` taking the `svc` path, and optional `MyHandleStubHijack` emulator exit path. |
| Logic Risk | This is a high-blast-radius assembly boundary: register volatility, stack alignment, `SBIELOW_DATA` offsets, syscall index handoff, and Arm64EC thunk routing are all part of the legal shape. Source review did not expose a surgical defect, so changing bytes would be worse than recording the ABI contract. Future changes must be driven by Windows ARM64 / ARM64EC runtime evidence, not by x64/x86 analogy. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-132-low-arm64-entry-syscall-abi-contract.md` records Microsoft Windows ARM64 ABI, Arm64EC ABI, and Arm64EC interoperability references. `docs/plan/srev-132-low-arm64-entry-syscall-abi-contract.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `LOW_ARM64_ENTRY_SYSCALL_ABI_CONTRACT` contract. |
| Fix | No source behavior changed. `Sandboxie/core/low/entry_arm.asm` is now ledger-named with its ARM64 / ARM64EC syscall bridge contract and source gate. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-132.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, ARM64 exported labels, `SystemServiceARM64` spill/parameter-block/call topology, `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC` sentinel and `MyHandleStubHijack` topology, `PrepSyscalls` ARM64EC patch edges, `SBIELOW_DATA` offset evidence, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-132.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows ARM64 lowlevel build, ARM64 sandbox process startup proving `SystemServiceARM64` stack and status behavior, ARM64EC process startup proving `NtDeviceIoControlFileEC` and `EcExitThunkPtr` routing, syscall hook smoke proving original `x0-x7` arguments reach `API_INVOKE_SYSCALL`, and disabled-hook smoke proving no ARM64EC wrapper state is used when `is_arm64ec` is false. |

### SREV-133: Low x64 Entry Nonvolatile Register

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official Windows x64 calling-convention review; needs Windows x64 lowlevel runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/low/entry_asm.asm` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-132. The x64 `_Start` prelude saves incoming `rcx`, `rdx`, `r8`, and `r9`, computes position-independent pointers for `SbieLowData`, `_DetourCode`, and `_SystemService`, calls `EntrypointC`, restores those four argument registers, then jumps to the `LdrInitializeThunk` trampoline returned in `rax`. Before this SREV, `_Start` used `rbx` as a scratch copy of the call/pop base address without saving or restoring it before the trampoline jump. Microsoft documents the x64 calling convention as passing integer arguments in `RCX`, `RDX`, `R8`, and `R9`, treats `RAX`, `RCX`, `RDX`, `R8`, `R9`, `R10`, and `R11` as volatile, and treats `RBX`, `RBP`, `RDI`, `RSI`, `RSP`, and `R12`-`R15` as nonvolatile registers that must be saved and restored by a function that uses them. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/low/entry_asm.asm`, `Sandboxie/core/low/init.c`, x64 `_Start`, `_001`, `SbieLowData`, `_DetourCode`, `_SystemService`, `EntrypointC`, original `rcx`, `rdx`, `r8`, `r9`, trampoline return in `rax`, scratch base register, `rbx`, `r10`, x64 volatile register set, x64 nonvolatile register set, and `LdrInitializeThunk` trampoline. |
| Schema | `LOW_X64_ENTRY_NONVOLATILE_REGISTER` says `_Start` is a detour prelude and must not clobber x64 nonvolatile registers before jumping to the `LdrInitializeThunk` trampoline; Windows x64 treats `RBX`, `RBP`, `RDI`, `RSI`, `RSP`, and `R12`-`R15` as nonvolatile; Windows x64 treats `RAX`, `RCX`, `RDX`, `R8`, `R9`, `R10`, and `R11` as volatile; `_Start` may use volatile scratch registers while preparing `EntrypointC` arguments; `_Start` preserves original `RCX`, `RDX`, `R8`, and `R9` across `EntrypointC`; `_Start` computes `SbieLowData`, `_DetourCode`, and `_SystemService` relative to the call/pop base; `_Start` must not use `RBX` as an unbalanced scratch register; `SystemService` x64 keeps its own `RBX` and `RDI` save/restore contract unchanged; x86 `_Start` and `DetourCode` behavior are unchanged. |
| Topology | Legal x64 entry flow is patched `LdrInitializeThunk` entry, `_Start`, save original `rcx` / `rdx` / `r8` / `r9`, call/pop base into a volatile scratch register, derive `SbieLowData`, `_DetourCode`, and `_SystemService`, call `EntrypointC`, restore original argument registers, and jump to the returned trampoline. The nonvolatile boundary is that `_Start` may clobber volatile scratch registers but must not leave `RBX`, `RBP`, `RDI`, `RSI`, or `R12`-`R15` changed. |
| Logic Risk | Lowlevel entry code runs before the original loader entry continues. It calls `EntrypointC` and then transfers control to the original trampoline, so an unbalanced nonvolatile-register clobber can corrupt loader or caller state under specific startup/register pressure. The correct local repair is to use a volatile scratch register for the call/pop base, not to add stack state or change the lowlevel section layout. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-133-low-x64-entry-nonvolatile-register.md` records Microsoft x64 calling-convention and ABI references. `docs/plan/srev-133-low-x64-entry-nonvolatile-register.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `LOW_X64_ENTRY_NONVOLATILE_REGISTER` contract. |
| Fix | The x64 `_Start` path now uses `r10` instead of `rbx` to hold the call/pop base while deriving `SbieLowData`, `_DetourCode`, and `_SystemService`. No x86 path, `EntrypointC` signature, section layout, detour target, syscall bridge, or `SystemService` save/restore logic changed. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-133.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, x64 `_Start` argument-save topology, volatile `r10` base use, stale unbalanced `rbx` scratch removal, original argument restore before trampoline jump, unchanged x86 `_Start`, unchanged `SystemService` `rbx` / `rdi` save/restore, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-133.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows x64 lowlevel build, x64 sandbox process startup proving the `LdrInitializeThunk` trampoline receives original `rcx`, `rdx`, `r8`, and `r9`, debugger smoke proving `rbx` before `_Start` matches `rbx` at the trampoline jump, x64 syscall hook smoke proving `SystemService` still receives `SbieLowData`, and x86 startup smoke proving 32-bit behavior is unchanged. |

### SREV-134: DriverAssist Clipboard Probe HGLOBAL Ownership

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| Severity | [major] |
| Status | patched source-level after official GlobalAlloc / GlobalLock / SetClipboardData / EmptyClipboard review; needs Windows clipboard runtime proof |
| Evidence | `Sandboxie/core/svc/DriverAssistStart.cpp` was the highest-ranked unnamed reviewable core file after SREV-133. `DriverAssist::InitClipboard` creates dummy clipboard entries on Vista+ so driver-side `Gui_InitClipboard` can infer the internal clipboard item layout. `Sandboxie/core/drv/gui.c` says the service placed four unique clipboard items, then scans the kernel clipboard item array for format sequence `0x111111`, `0x222222`, `0x333333`, and `0x444444`. Before this SREV, the service created only two movable memory objects, reused each object for two clipboard formats, and dereferenced `GlobalLock` returns without checking `NULL`. Microsoft documents `GlobalAlloc` as returning `NULL` on failure; documents `GlobalLock` as returning a pointer and `NULL` on failure; documents `SetClipboardData` as placing a memory object on the clipboard with movable-memory requirements for memory objects; and documents `EmptyClipboard` as emptying the clipboard only while it is open. |
| Data | `Sandboxie/core/svc/DriverAssistStart.cpp`, `Sandboxie/core/drv/gui.c`, `DriverAssist::InitClipboard`, `Formats[4]`, four `HGLOBAL` probe objects, `GlobalAlloc`, `GlobalLock`, `GlobalUnlock`, `SetClipboardData`, `OpenClipboard`, `EmptyClipboard`, `CloseClipboard`, `GlobalFree`, `API_GUI_CLIPBOARD`, driver-side `Gui_InitClipboard`, and probe formats `0x111111`, `0x222222`, `0x333333`, `0x444444`. |
| Schema | `DRIVERASSIST_CLIPBOARD_PROBE_HGLOBAL_OWNERSHIP` says `InitClipboard` creates four unique movable memory objects for the four driver-probe clipboard formats; `GlobalAlloc` or `GlobalLock` failure prevents clipboard probing and still releases every allocated local object; `InitClipboard` dereferences a `GlobalLock` pointer only after it is non-null; `SetClipboardData` receives one unique `HGLOBAL` per private probe format; `API_GUI_CLIPBOARD` runs only after dummy clipboard items have been placed; the clipboard is emptied after the driver probe and before local dummy-object release; `Gui_InitClipboard` still observes the same four format markers in the same order; Vista+ probing and retry topology are unchanged. |
| Topology | Legal flow is Vista+ service startup, allocate four movable dummy `HGLOBAL` objects, lock/check/init/unlock each object, retry `OpenClipboard`, `EmptyClipboard`, place formats `0x111111` / `0x222222` / `0x333333` / `0x444444`, call `API_GUI_CLIPBOARD`, `EmptyClipboard`, `CloseClipboard`, then release local dummy objects. Driver observation remains `Gui_InitClipboard`, reference clipboard, require at least four items, scan the four format ids, and derive item length plus integrity index. |
| Logic Risk | The service and driver sides disagreed on the probe data shape: driver comments and scanner expect four unique dummy items, but the service reused two memory objects. Reusing clipboard memory objects makes ownership ambiguous around `SetClipboardData` and `EmptyClipboard`; unchecked `GlobalLock` can also crash the service under allocation or handle-state failure. The correct local repair is to make the service probe shape match the driver observation shape with four independent dummy objects and checked lock pointers. |
| Official Shape | `docs/plan/srev-134-driverassist-clipboard-probe-hglobal-ownership.md` records Microsoft `GlobalAlloc`, `GlobalLock`, `SetClipboardData`, and `EmptyClipboard` references. `docs/plan/srev-134-driverassist-clipboard-probe-hglobal-ownership.schema.json` records the JSON Schema draft-07 local `DRIVERASSIST_CLIPBOARD_PROBE_HGLOBAL_OWNERSHIP` contract. |
| Fix | `DriverAssist::InitClipboard` now allocates four `GMEM_MOVEABLE` `HGLOBAL` objects, initializes each only after `GlobalLock` returns a non-null pointer, and calls `SetClipboardData` once per format using the matching unique object. If allocation or lock initialization fails, the clipboard probe is skipped and every allocated dummy object is released. |
| Acceptance Gate | `docs/plan/check-srev-134.py` validates the draft-07 schema, official references, four unique dummy `HGLOBAL` slots, checked `GlobalAlloc` and `GlobalLock` topology, one `SetClipboardData` call per probe format through the `Formats` array, unchanged `API_GUI_CLIPBOARD` / `EmptyClipboard` / `CloseClipboard` order, driver-side four-format scanner evidence, and ledger entry; `docs/plan/check-srev-134.sh` is the matrix wrapper. Runtime/build gate: Windows service build for `DriverAssistStart.cpp`, Vista+ clipboard init smoke proving `API_GUI_CLIPBOARD` detects item length and integrity index, allocation failure injection proving no `GlobalLock` NULL dereference and no dummy object leak, clipboard-open retry smoke proving unopened clipboard leaves every local object freed, and ordinary clipboard operations proving integrity correction still works. |
